## [FLARE Uses Line-Level Suspiciousness to Guide LLM Code Refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03852) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers present FLARE, an iterative framework built around a lightweight diagnostic model that predicts line-level suspiciousness to localize bugs and guide LLM code refinement. On LiveCodeBench and BigCodeBench with five base LLMs, FLARE beats the strongest baseline by 1.72% to 7.42% even without candidate search (k=1), and searching over 10 candidates adds an average 8.50% improvement. Existing refinement signals such as test failures and self-critiques are too coarse or too high-level to tell a model where to fix a bug, so FLARE's fine-grained localization could make AI-assisted debugging and program repair more reliable. This matters for developers and tooling vendors building LLM-based coding assistants, since better localization directly translates into fewer wasted iterations. Because diagnostic predictions are inherently uncertain, FLARE searches over the top-k suspicious regions and selects the best candidate based on execution outcomes. Evaluated in isolation, the lightweight diagnostic model also outperforms recent fault localization methods, though the approach still depends on execution feedback to pick among candidates.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models frequently generate buggy code, and common repair loops feed back signals like failing test cases or the model's own critique. These signals usually indicate that something is wrong without pinpointing which lines are responsible, which is the classic fault localization problem. LiveCodeBench is a contamination-free benchmark built from freshly collected programming contest problems, while BigCodeBench evaluates LLMs on practical, challenging programming tasks.

## [ASRD: Training-Free Revocable Decoding for Diffusion LLMs via Anchor Tokens](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16847) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose ASRD (Anchor Supervised Revocable Decoding), a training-free framework that operates in the embedding space to improve revocable decoding for diffusion large language models (dLLMs). It decouples the decoding context into trusted anchor tokens, identified via temporal consistency, and uncertain candidates, using a dynamic Anchor Tokens Cache with two mechanisms: Anchor-Guided Generation and Anchor-Perturbed Verification. Experiments on math and coding benchmarks show accuracy gains up to 6.4% and inference throughput speedups up to 7.2x over recent remasking baselines. Diffusion LLMs promise parallel generation but suffer from a speed-quality trade-off, and existing revocable decoding methods are plagued by error propagation and local error reinforcement. ASRD's training-free approach could significantly improve decoding quality and speed, making dLLMs more practical for real-world math and coding tasks, and it contributes to the rapidly growing field of diffusion language models. ASRD is training-free and operates in the embedding space, using temporal consistency to identify anchor tokens and a dynamic cache to guide generation and verification. The two mechanisms are Anchor-Guided Generation, which injects entropy-weighted anchor signals into masked positions to rectify attention toward a reliable global skeleton, and Anchor-Perturbed Verification, which applies orthogonal perturbations to uncertain candidates to destabilize and remask errors from fragile local consensus. Code is available at https://github.com/preordinary/ASRD.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion large language models (dLLMs) generate text by iteratively refining masked tokens in parallel, unlike autoregressive models that generate left-to-right. This parallel generation can be faster but often produces lower-quality outputs, and revocable decoding strategies try to fix errors by verifying and remasking tokens. However, these strategies operate in a mixed-quality context where errors can propagate and reinforce each other, making it hard to detect and correct them.

## [SegTME-UNI2 unifies cell segmentation and LLM-driven tumor microenvironment reporting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17702) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers released SegTME-UNI2, an end-to-end framework that combines the UNI2 pathology foundation model with dual UperNet decoders (UNI2-UperHoVer) for six-class semantic segmentation and HV gradient regression, enabling watershed-based nuclear instance separation. The pipeline computes 22 per-patch TME metrics, converts them into six categorical phenotype labels and a standardized biological-token vocabulary fine-tuned via NVIDIA BioNeMo, and generates clinician-readable narratives; the pseudo-labeled TCGA-UT dataset and UNI2-UperHoVer checkpoints are publicly released. This work addresses a real clinical need by integrating segmentation, quantitative feature extraction, and interpretable reporting into a single pipeline, potentially reducing the manual burden of tumor microenvironment analysis in digital pathology. The public release of the TCGA-UT dataset and model checkpoints could accelerate large-scale spatial biology research and lower the barrier for other groups to build on foundation-model-based pathology workflows. The model is trained via a three-stage progressive pseudo-label curriculum, scaling from PanNuke at 0.25 µm/pixel to TCGA-UT Scale-0 at 0.5 µm/pixel and finally to a 1.6M-patch, six-scale TCGA-UT dataset at 0.5–1.0 µm/pixel. Qualitative validation on IGNITE NSCLC tiles shows biologically coherent phenotype classifications and narratives despite inter-institutional stain variability and imperfect segmentation, though the work remains qualitative rather than a large-scale quantitative benchmark.

## [Subjective Risk Decomposition Unifies Uncertainty Quantification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15196) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2607.15196v3) proposes that epistemic and aleatoric uncertainty measures are not primitives requiring axioms, but consequences of decomposing a subjective risk based on a strictly proper loss. The authors show that reverse cross entropy recovers classic information-theoretic uncertainty terms, and that the same approach recovers numerous existing UQ measures, extending the view to learning theory via subjective risk analogues of excess risk, approximation error, and estimation error. This work offers a common theoretical foundation for many previously disparate uncertainty quantification measures, potentially simplifying how researchers choose and justify UQ methods. By linking UQ to learning theory through subjective risk, it could influence future research in uncertainty quantification, learning theory, and information-theoretic machine learning. The framework hinges on strictly proper losses, where the target probability vector is the unique minimizer, and reverse cross entropy serves as a prominent example that recovers classic information-theoretic terms. The authors frame this as a first step toward a full learning-theoretic framework for uncertainty quantification, noting that the induced epistemic and aleatoric terms depend on the chosen modelling scenario and loss.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Uncertainty quantification (UQ) in machine learning typically distinguishes epistemic uncertainty, which arises from a lack of knowledge and can be reduced with more data, from aleatoric uncertainty, which is inherent randomness that cannot be reduced. Strictly proper losses are loss functions whose unique minimizer is the true probability vector, and examples include the logarithmic score and the Brier score. Reverse cross entropy is a training method related to cross entropy that has been used, for instance, to learn more distinguishable feature representations for detecting adversarial examples.

## [Doctoral Thesis Proposes Unified Framework for Riemannian Deep Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19305) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A doctoral thesis (arXiv:2607.19305v4) develops a unified framework for Riemannian deep learning across three perspectives: reusable neural modules, manifold-specific network architectures, and geometry design. It generalizes batch normalization from Euclidean spaces and individual manifolds to broad classes of Lie groups and gyrogroups, and extends multinomial logistic regression from Euclidean space to SPD manifolds and then to general Riemannian manifolds. This work addresses a core fragmentation problem in geometric deep learning, where basic components are often tied to specific manifolds or rely on numerically fragile Euclidean approximations. By providing reusable modules and efficient geometries, it could lower the barrier to building neural networks on manifold-valued data in vision, signal processing, graph learning, and genomics. The thesis develops an unconstrained model of hyperbolic space, Busemann-based hyperbolic learning, and networks for full-rank correlation matrices, and introduces adaptive, computationally efficient Riemannian metrics on SPD manifolds including learnable Log-Euclidean geometries and fast, stable Cholesky-based geometries. The proposed methods are supported by theoretical analysis and validated through numerical experiments and applications.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Riemannian deep learning extends standard neural networks to data that naturally live on curved spaces (manifolds), such as symmetric positive definite (SPD) matrices, hyperbolic spaces, and correlation matrices. Lie groups and gyrogroups are algebraic structures that describe symmetries and non-associative group-like operations, which are useful for defining operations like batch normalization on manifolds. SPD manifolds are widely used to represent covariance and diffusion tensor data, while hyperbolic space is well suited for hierarchical or tree-like data.

A new arXiv paper (2607.20002v3) presents a unifying framework that categorizes post-training methods for time series foundation models (TSFMs) into five intervention loci: parameter adaptation, context augmentation, model composition, output processing and uncertainty control, and compression and specialization. The survey analyzes representative methods in each category, discusses their limitations, and outlines future directions such as controlled adaptation and deployment-aware specialization. As TSFMs like TimesFM and MOMENT proliferate, pretraining alone is often insufficient for reliable downstream deployment, so this taxonomy gives researchers and practitioners a shared vocabulary and design map for adapting these models. It could accelerate progress in a rapidly growing area by clarifying trade-offs and highlighting open problems in post-training. The framework organizes methods by where they intervene in the prediction pipeline, covering techniques such as parameter-efficient adaptation (e.g., LoRA-style updates), context construction, model composition, calibration, and compression. The paper is a survey rather than a new empirical breakthrough, and it explicitly notes limitations within each category while proposing five future research directions.

## [CloudEdgeVLA Enables Latency-Tolerant Robot Control via Cloud-Edge Collaboration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.00569) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CloudEdgeVLA introduces a cloud-edge collaborative policy for Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models that treats temporal misalignment as a representation-learning problem. By training with paired current and randomly delayed frames targeting the same action, it achieves 63.8-78.0% success on four LIBERO suites under a 40-step uniform-delay window, while the VLASH baseline reaches at most 6.4% and single-path baselines at most 3.0%. This work addresses a core systems conflict in deploying billion-parameter VLA policies on mobile robots: cloud GPUs provide semantic reasoning but introduce network delay, while closed-loop control demands local responsiveness. By removing blocking synchronization from the control loop, it offers a practical path to scalable VLA deployment where cloud models can grow while edge computation stays lightweight. The cloud VLA encodes delayed observations into slowly varying task features, while a lightweight edge head fuses the latest available cloud feature with current local vision, driving emergent specialization between the two paths. The approach is evaluated across four LIBERO suites with a 40-step uniform-delay window, and notably requires no explicit scheduling or delay cues.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

Deploying such billion-parameter models on mobile robots is challenging because semantic reasoning benefits from cloud GPUs, but closed-loop control must respond locally despite network delay and jitter. Existing hierarchical and asynchronous policies improve throughput, yet their slow-path representations can arrive stale or require explicit scheduling and delay cues.

## [DDRSR generalizes decomposition and variable reduction in symbolic regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.02628) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.02628v3) introduces Deep Divide-and-Reduce in Symbolic Regression (DDRSR), a mathematically grounded framework that generalizes the decomposition and variable-reduction paradigm of AI Feynman. DDRSR extends translational symmetry to coefficient- and exponent-interfered forms, enables variable separation under overlapping variables and additive constant offsets, and generalizes the identification of nested compositional structures. Symbolic regression is a key route to interpretable machine learning and automated scientific discovery, but existing learning-based methods rarely exploit the structural mathematical properties of expressions. By broadening the range of decomposable structures beyond AI Feynman, DDRSR improves downstream regression accuracy and exact-expression recovery, which could benefit researchers in interpretable ML and data-driven scientific discovery. DDRSR generalizes translational symmetry to coefficient- and exponent-interfered forms and handles variable separation under overlapping variables and additive constant offsets, while also generalizing nested compositional structure identification. The paper further characterizes an intrinsic non-identifiability limitation of decomposition when no effective variable separation is induced, and experiments across multiple SR algorithms and benchmark datasets show broader decomposable-structure identification than AI Feynman.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Symbolic regression (SR) searches the space of mathematical expressions to find a model that best fits data while remaining interpretable, and it is known to be NP-hard. AI Feynman introduced a physics-inspired paradigm that recursively decomposes complex expressions by exploiting structural properties such as translational symmetry, but its decomposition criteria cover only restricted structural forms and its handling of nested composition can require brute-force search over candidate sub-expressions. DDRSR builds on this paradigm to systematically generalize expression decomposition and variable reduction.

## [Hierarchical VLA Model Uses Explicit Language Memory for Long-Horizon Robot Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04765) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2608.04765v2) proposes a hierarchical vision-language-action architecture that converts discrete temporal observations into an explicit textual memory sequence with temporal logic. The system decouples a high-level VLM, trained via a visual question answering paradigm, from a low-level VLA controller, and the high-level VLM recursively updates both the language memory and subtask instructions using the previous memory as a contextual anchor. Long-horizon tasks remain a major bottleneck for VLA models because sparse demonstrations, non-Markovian dependencies, and accumulated execution errors degrade temporal consistency. An explicit, interpretable language memory could improve success rates and robustness in complex robotic manipulation, and the sim-to-real validation suggests the approach may transfer beyond simulation. The architecture addresses four specific problems: sparse expert demonstrations limiting cross-task compositional generalization, non-Markovian task structure, limited closed-loop error correction, and end-to-end action fine-tuning weakening the VLM backbone's semantic representations. Evaluation was conducted in multiple simulation environments plus sim-to-real experiments on a real robotic platform, though the abstract is truncated and no peer review or community discussion is yet available.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

Long-horizon planning refers to tasks requiring many sequential steps and sub-goals, where the non-Markovian nature means the optimal action depends on the entire interaction history rather than only the current observation. Explicit memory mechanisms aim to give such policies a persistent record of past states and decisions so they can maintain temporal consistency and correct errors during execution.

## [Verifiability, Not Capability, Is the Binding Constraint for Agentic AI in FinTech](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11344) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2608.11344v3) introduces the concept of the "Verifiability Gap" — the shortfall between the verification that delegated authority demands and the explainability and reproducibility retained after a decision — and tests its mechanisms across three studies covering nine model versions, from a 3-billion-parameter local model to a commercial frontier system. The paper argues that the binding governance constraint for agentic AI in FinTech is verifiability rather than capability, and proposes a multilevel governance theory built around evidence-contingent delegation. As financial institutions delegate consequential decisions to agentic AI systems that decompose goals, coordinate models and tools, and act with little oversight, this paper reframes the governance debate away from raw capability benchmarks toward auditability and reproducibility. If verifiability is indeed the binding constraint, regulators, auditors, and model providers may need to shift their focus to retained evidence, replay controls, and audit lag rather than simply measuring how capable a model is. Study 1 found that provider releases alter historical financial actions and that the controls needed for replay belong to the provider: the frontier model rejects temperature, top_p and top_k outright and exposes no random seed, while under the tightest available controls a local model reproduced 320 of 320 executions, hosted models 319 of 320 and 959 of 960. Study 2 showed orchestration acts as a latent policy layer, with no execution record repeating in any configuration at any scale, and Study 3 showed two deterministic credit-model versions each reproduce their current action perfectly yet cannot recover a historical one.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic AI refers to systems that autonomously decompose goals, coordinate multiple models and tools, and act with limited human oversight — a step beyond single-turn chatbots. In FinTech, such systems may make credit, trading, or compliance decisions, raising questions about who is accountable and whether a decision can be audited after the fact. Reproducibility in machine learning typically depends on controlling sampling parameters such as temperature, top_p, and top_k, which govern how randomly a model selects its outputs; if a provider hides or rejects these controls, independent replay of a decision becomes difficult. The paper's "Verifiability Gap" is indexed to a verifier, an evidentiary standard, and an audit lag, meaning it is not a single number but depends on who is checking, against what standard, and how long after the decision.

## [Unsupervised Anomaly Detection Benchmark for Multi-Center Breast MRI Quality Assurance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16725) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.16725v2) introduces a controlled benchmark of 17 realistic QA-relevant anomaly types drawn from six public datasets, plus a taxonomy of radiological image anomalies based on human visual perception. It evaluates four unsupervised anomaly/OOD detection methods on multi-center dynamic contrast-enhanced breast MRI, with the projection-based method using a domain-specific feature extractor and novel positional encoding reaching the highest AUROC of 0.954, while a 3D reconstruction-based approach achieves 0.936 with better generalization to unseen institutions. Corrupted or inconsistent data silently threatens the safety of medical AI, and regulators increasingly recognize dataset quality assurance for high-risk applications, yet scalable automated detection remains underdeveloped. This benchmark and taxonomy provide practical guidance for building automated QA into medical AI pipelines, and the finding that hybrid methods validated on one modality or anatomy fail without domain-specific adaptation warns against naive method reuse. The benchmark spans near-, medium-far-, and far-OOD samples as well as in-distribution and external normal data; medium-far and far-OOD are detected reliably, but near-OOD and external normal data from unseen institutions expose method-specific differences. Both unmodified hybrid OOD methods exhibit critical failure modes, and implants and mastectomies remain an open challenge for all four methods.

Dynamic contrast-enhanced breast MRI is an established high-sensitivity imaging method for detecting breast lesions, and multi-center studies pool scans from different institutions, scanners, and protocols, which makes dataset quality control difficult. ## [SARA Attack Defeats Token Shuffling Privacy in ViT Split Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.01232) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper introduces the Spatially Aligned Reconstruction Attack (SARA), a unified pipeline that predicts token positions, restores spatial layout, reconstructs missing embeddings with a feature-space masked autoencoder, and recovers the original input image from ViT split-inference transmissions. The work shows that token shuffling provides only apparent privacy, while token reduction offers stronger but still incomplete protection, and proposes a lightweight edge-side defense that removes positional embeddings and adapts edge transformer blocks via knowledge distillation. This research challenges the assumed privacy benefits of token reduction and shuffling in split-inference systems, which are increasingly deployed for edge computing where resource-constrained devices offload computation to cloud servers. It highlights that transmitted intermediate representations can leak substantial visual information, urging practitioners to adopt stronger defenses when designing privacy-preserving distributed inference pipelines. SARA operates in four stages: predicting token positions, restoring spatial layout, reconstructing missing embeddings using a feature-space masked autoencoder, and recovering the input image. The proposed edge-side defense removes positional embeddings and progressively adapts edge-side transformer blocks through knowledge distillation, substantially reducing attack performance while preserving downstream task accuracy and requiring no changes to the cloud-side model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Vision Transformers (ViTs) split an image into patches, embed them as tokens, and process them with self-attention, making token representations a compact summary of visual content. In split inference, an edge device runs the first few layers and sends intermediate tokens to a cloud server for the remaining computation, which saves local resources but risks exposing the tokens to feature inversion attacks that try to reconstruct the original input. Token reduction drops less important tokens to cut communication costs, and token shuffling reorders tokens to disrupt spatial structure, both assumed to hinder reconstruction. This paper tests those assumptions and finds that positional information persists in the embeddings, enabling a new attack.

## [OmniRSCLIP Extends CLIP to Multi-Source Remote Sensing Sensors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.03391) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose OmniRSCLIP, an end-to-end contrastive learning framework that extends CLIP beyond its fixed RGB input interface to support heterogeneous remote sensing sensors such as SAR, MSI, and HSI. The work introduces Spectral-Spatial Basis Decomposition (SSBD), a spectral-context-aware mask-based contrastive learning scheme, and OmniRS5M, described as the first large-scale remote sensing image-text corpus covering RGB, SAR, MSI, and HSI. Most remote sensing vision-language models are built on RGB-oriented CLIP architectures, which limits their ability to exploit the rich spectral information captured by SAR, multi-spectral, and hyperspectral sensors. By aligning these heterogeneous modalities in a unified image-text semantic space while preserving RGB performance, OmniRSCLIP could broaden the applicability of CLIP-style pretraining across the remote sensing and multimodal learning communities. SSBD formulates arbitrary-channel adaptation as a basis recomposition problem: pretrained CLIP patch embeddings serve as transferable spatial bases, while wavelength-conditioned coefficients span sensor-specific embedding kernels within a constrained visual prior space. Experiments on retrieval, zero-shot classification, and semantic localization show that OmniRSCLIP preserves strong RGB-domain performance while effectively extending CLIP to heterogeneous remote sensing modalities.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: CLIP (Contrastive Language-Image Pre-training) is a technique that trains paired image and text encoders with a contrastive objective, enabling zero-shot transfer to tasks such as cross-modal retrieval and text-to-image generation. Standard CLIP models are pretrained on RGB images with three input channels, so they cannot directly ingest the many spectral bands produced by SAR, multi-spectral imaging (MSI), and hyperspectral imaging (HSI). Hyperspectral imaging captures spatial and spectral information simultaneously across hundreds of narrow wavelength bands, making it valuable for material characterization but incompatible with RGB-oriented architectures.

## [PACE Uses Proprioception to Anchor Cross-Modal Pretraining for Sim-to-Real Assembly](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.07534) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers Yuhan Wang, Yurou Chen, Hongye Jiang, and Wenzhao Lian present PACE (Proprioception-Anchored Cross-Modal Encoder), a pretraining method that supervises temporal visual and force/torque representations by predicting proprioceptive state transitions. Across four contact-rich assembly tasks, policies trained on frozen PACE features achieve an average real-world success rate of 93.3% with only a 2.7-percentage-point sim-to-real drop, deployed directly on hardware without real-world fine-tuning or object-pose tracking. Contact-rich assembly demands submillimeter accuracy and reliable force interpretation, and sim-to-real gaps in visual observations, contact dynamics, and force/torque measurements have long limited policy transfer. By exploiting proprioception as a domain-consistent anchor, PACE offers a practical route to zero-shot deployment that could reduce the need for costly real-world fine-tuning in industrial robotic assembly. The method works because joint positions are expressed in a shared calibrated coordinate system and joint velocities are computed consistently in simulation and on hardware, so static domain-specific factors such as lighting, texture, and sensor bias carry little information about joint motion and are suppressed by the objective. PACE remains robust to perturbations that substantially degrade pose-based and learned-fusion baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Proprioception in robotics refers to a robot's ability to sense its own position and movement in space without relying on vision, which is crucial for interacting with the environment. Sim-to-real transfer is a machine learning paradigm in which a policy is trained extensively in simulation and then deployed on physical hardware, but discrepancies between simulated and real sensors, dynamics, and visuals often cause performance drops. Cross-modal pretraining aims to learn shared representations across modalities such as vision, touch, and force, and PACE extends this idea by using proprioception as the anchoring signal.

## [Adaptive Anisotropic Attention Factorizes Attention for EEG Signals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.08788) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces Adaptive Anisotropic Attention (AAA), which splits attention into a temporal path (within each electrode across time) and a spatial path (across electrodes at the same time step), combined by a learned per-token gate. The resulting model, AXON (AXis-factorized Operator Network), improves mean balanced accuracy over a dense baseline on six EEG downstream tasks under both linear probing and full fine-tuning. The work challenges the isotropic prior of dense self-attention, showing that aligning attention with the natural axes of structured signals provides a useful inductive bias. If it generalizes, this could benefit other low-SNR, axis-structured domains such as audio spectrograms, which the authors test in controlled experiments. Both the temporal and spatial paths are shown to be necessary, and the learned convex combination outperforms a hard choice of a single path; most of the benefit comes from the gate learning a different temporal/spatial balance at each layer. The abstract does not report the magnitude of improvements or comparisons against state-of-the-art EEG models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: EEG (electroencephalography) records brain activity via multiple electrodes over time, producing signals with low signal-to-noise ratio whose dependencies are organized along electrode and time axes. Standard Transformer self-attention treats all token pairs as equally plausible before learning, an isotropic prior that can expose each token to many irrelevant interactions. AAA instead factorizes attention along these two axes, and AXON applies it to two-dimensional EEG tokens representing patches from 21 canonical 10-20 electrodes and 11 temporal positions in a 10-second window.

## [Atomic User Model Enables Personality-Aware LLM Interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.12086) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.12086v2) proposes the Atomic User Model (AUM), a layered, human-readable personality profile for LLM assistants, and identifies an effect called 'personality seepage' where a prompt's wording transfers the writer's personality to the model. Using AUM as a searchable index, eight retrieved fields matched full-profile writing quality while using only 23% of the context (211 vs 915 tokens), and accuracy in identifying a user's own writing rose from 14.9% to 42.7%. Current personalization systems summarize user preferences, which are task-dependent and force the model to relearn the user whenever the task changes; AUM instead captures a stable identity core, potentially making LLM assistants more consistent and personalized across tasks. The finding of personality seepage also raises new concerns about unintended personality transfer in prompt engineering and human-computer interaction. The AUM organizes a person as a stable identity nucleus surrounded by four interpretable shells (psychological, cognitive/experiential, behavioral, and social) plus cross-shell entries for inner conflict and authenticity; a task classifier, selection step, and budgeted retriever pass only a few relevant fields to the model. The pipeline was tested with 16 simulated users, 6 style-sensitive tasks, and 3 seeds, and four pre-registered controls showed no effect, indicating the gain comes from the profile's structure rather than the search method.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Large language model assistants are increasingly expected to write in their users' own voice, and most current systems do this by summarizing user preferences and inserting that summary into the prompt. However, preferences are only the surface of a person and change with the task, while underlying personality is more stable, so preference-only storage means the model must relearn the user whenever the task changes. This paper is an arXiv preprint and has not yet undergone peer review.

## [ASTRIL-MPC: Language-Guided Neural MPC for Articulated Tracked Robots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13083) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers present ASTRIL-MPC, a framework that combines a learned kinematics model, nonlinear model predictive control (NMPC), and safety-checked LLM-driven weight updates to autonomously traverse contact-rich urban search-and-rescue environments. It improves an aggregate traversal-quality score by up to 71% over non-adaptive NMPC and 67% over a PPO baseline, while eliminating measurable collision impacts during descent, with a full control cycle running within 100 ms. Articulated tracked robots are widely used in urban search-and-rescue, but their flipper-track coordination and hybrid, discontinuous robot-terrain interaction are hard to model analytically, making reliable autonomy difficult. This work shows that combining terrain-conditioned neural kinematics with optimization-based planning and language-guided adaptation can yield data-efficient, robust autonomy that transfers to real contact-rich physical traversal. The learned kinematics model predicts short-horizon task-state increments from a height sequence and recent trajectories, while the LLM proposes only bounded updates to selected weights and bounds through an interface with range clipping, rate limiting, and consistency checks. The compiled predictor enables a full control cycle within 100 ms, and real-robot trials over four indoor obstacles demonstrate transfer to physical contact-rich traversal.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Model predictive control (MPC) is an optimization-based control method that plans over a short future horizon while respecting constraints, and neural MPC replaces or augments parts of the dynamics model with learned neural networks. Articulated tracked robots (ATRs) have a main body plus articulated flippers that help them climb stairs and cross rubble, but coordinating flippers and tracks over hybrid, discontinuous terrain is analytically difficult. Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly explored as high-level advisors in robotics, and here they are used to tune controller weights rather than to directly output motor commands.

## [DiffAdapterVLA Embeds Trajectory Planning Inside Driving VLM Backbone](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15322) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.15322v2) introduces DiffAdapterVLA, which injects explicit trajectory tokens into selected late layers of a pretrained driving vision-language model so that trajectory state co-evolves with driving conditions during backbone forward computation. Lightweight layer-wise DiffAdapters perform recursive trajectory refinement, and an asymmetric joint attention mechanism preserves directed guidance from the condition stream to planning, yielding high-quality closed-loop planning on NAVSIM with low end-to-end latency and few trainable parameters. Most existing driving VLM pipelines generate trajectories only after the model has formed a final condition, which separates depth-wise condition computation from the stepwise formation of trajectory state. By moving planning into the backbone itself and adapting only lightweight modules, DiffAdapterVLA suggests a more parameter-efficient path to continuous planning that could influence how end-to-end autonomous driving systems are architected. The method adapts only lightweight trajectory modules on top of existing driving priors rather than training an independent trajectory planner, and it reports low end-to-end latency with few trainable parameters on NAVSIM. As an arXiv preprint without peer review or community discussion, its claims have not yet been independently validated.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Driving vision-language models (VLMs) are pretrained models that combine visual, route, language, and driving context into rich driving priors, but their representation objectives are typically separate from continuous driving planning. Diffusion models have recently been used for trajectory generation because they can expressively model noised future trajectories together with conditional information. DiffAdapterVLA combines these ideas by inserting trajectory tokens directly into the VLM's late-layer computation instead of relying on a separate planner.

Researchers present CueNav, a video model-based navigation framework that combines visual cue guided video planning with an embodiment-specific Inverse-Dynamics Model (IDM). Using a Bird's-Eye View (BEV) map and retaining part of the robot body in the egocentric view as cues, CueNav achieves nearly 2x higher success in maze navigation and 70% success in a narrow passage where comparison methods largely fail. This work addresses two underexplored limitations in generative video planning for robotics: long-horizon planning and precise video-to-action translation. By showing zero-shot semantic-conditioned navigation and cross-platform deployment of the same video planner, it points toward a generalizable navigation framework that could reduce the need for task-specific retraining across different robots. The IDM translates dense flow fields extracted from the video plan into robot actions, and the body-aware view exposes embodiment context to the planner. The framework supports zero-shot semantic-conditioned navigation and deployment of the same video planner across different robot platforms, with code and additional results available on the project website.

## [OpenClaw Skill Registry Boom Fades, Study Warns Governance Gaps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17274) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.17274v2) empirically analyzes the OpenClaw AI agent skill ecosystem, showing that its public registry's observable stock nearly doubled in 91 days during early 2026 before monthly listing creation and core-repository activity fell from spring peaks. Drawing on OpenClaw Git history, GitHub issues and pull requests, and three ClawHub registry snapshots, the study finds that the top 10% of skills captured 46.93% of all downloads and that 85.06% of readable skills carry privilege evidence. The findings show that fast-growing agent-skill registries cannot be governed through simple metadata or a single scanner score, which matters for platform operators, security teams, and enterprises adopting AI agents at scale. The boom-bust pattern and attention concentration also offer a cautionary template for other open-source AI ecosystems racing to distribute natural-language agent skills. Human scrutiny did not keep pace: 77.86% of skills have zero stars and zero comments, and no simple skill features such as size or download counts remained a stable predictor of continued listing once creation cohort and skill age were controlled. Automated cleanup is also immature — three security scanners disagreed on 23,702 of the 61,990 skills they all cover, and after human adjudication, weighted scanner sensitivity against the reference standard ranged from just 21.67% to 61.06%.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: OpenClaw is a free and open-source autonomous AI agent that executes tasks via large language models and meets users in messaging platforms such as WhatsApp, Telegram, and Slack. Agent skills are natural-language instructions that direct a host agent toward shell, network, credential, file, and process actions, and public registries like ClawHub distribute them at scale. Because these skills can carry privileged operations, security scanners such as Cisco's Skill Scanner, Snyk's Agent Scan, and Mondoo's AI Agent Security Scanner have emerged to detect prompt injection, data exfiltration, and malicious code patterns.

The Chaos Computer Club has announced that the 40th Chaos Communication Congress (40C3) will take place from 27 to 30 December 2026 under the theme 'Model Citizens'. The Call for Participation for talks, entertainment, art, punk and music opened alongside the announcement. As Europe's largest hacker conference, the Congress is a key annual gathering for the security, privacy and digital-rights community, and its theme often frames the year's debates on technology and civil liberties. The announcement gives the global hacker community a focal point and an open call to help shape the programme. The event will run for four days from 27 to 30 December 2026, the dates established since 2005, and the Call for Participation invites submissions for talks as well as entertainment, art, punk and music. The CCC describes the Congress as a huge shared stage that attendees are invited to help shape.

hackernews · antonly · Sep 17, 08:03 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=49737787)

**Background**: The Chaos Computer Club (CCC) is Europe's largest association of hackers, founded in 1981 in Germany, with around 7,700 registered members and local chapters called Erfa-Kreise. It advocates transparency, freedom of information and the hacker ethic, and its members have exposed numerous information-security problems and served as expert witnesses before Germany's constitutional court. The Chaos Communication Congress has been held annually at the end of the year since 1984, and is considered one of the largest events of its kind alongside DEF CON in Las Vegas.

## [Datasette 1.0a40 adds background task API and httpx2 migration](https://simonwillison.net/2026/Sep/16/datasette/) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Datasette 1.0a40, an alpha pre-release, ships the same security fix as 0.65.5 along with a new datasette.add_background_task() method that lets plugins launch and manage background tasks, a migration of Datasette's internal HTTP client to httpx2, and a large batch of bug fixes from a 1.0 issue-triage effort. The background task API gives plugin authors a supported, first-class way to run long-lived work without tying it to an incoming HTTP request, which should simplify plugins like datasette-cron and datasette-enrichments that previously relied on asgi_wrapper hacks. The security fix and the steady bug-fixing toward a 1.0 stable release also matter for anyone running Datasette in production. The new datasette.add_background_task() method is documented in Datasette's internals docs, and plugins that currently start background tasks inside asgi_wrapper on the first incoming request are advised to migrate to it. The httpx2 migration affects internal features such as datasette.client.get(), and httpx2 is a Pydantic-maintained continuation of the original HTTPX client supporting HTTP/1.1, HTTP/2, and both sync and async APIs.

Plugins are third-party Python packages that hook into Datasette's internals, and background tasks are jobs that run independently of any single user request, such as scheduled imports or data enrichment.

## [Datasette 0.65.5 Fixes Table Permission Bypass via Trailing Newline](https://simonwillison.net/2026/Sep/16/datasette-2/) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Datasette 0.65.5 was released as a security patch for a vulnerability where a trailing newline in a requested table name could bypass table permissions and expose private rows. The issue was reported by researcher dpfkdlemtp under advisory GHSA-h547-rmjf-5m2m. Anyone running a Datasette instance with table-level permissions configured should upgrade immediately, since the flaw could leak rows that were meant to stay private. It is a reminder that seemingly trivial input normalization issues, like trailing whitespace, can undermine access-control logic in data publishing tools. The advisory notes that in the 1.0 alpha series, users with table creation and alteration permissions could also rename protected tables. The fix is included in the 0.65.5 patch release, and users on affected versions should upgrade to close the bypass.

rss · Simon Willison · Sep 16, 23:51

**Background**: Datasette is an open-source tool by Simon Willison that turns SQLite databases into queryable, shareable websites, and it is widely used by journalists and researchers to publish data. It supports fine-grained permissions, including rules that restrict which tables or rows a given user can see. This vulnerability involved a table name with a trailing newline being treated differently by permission checks than by the code that actually fetched the data, allowing protected rows to be returned.

## [PAC-2026 Protocol Makes AI-Assisted Claims Independently Challengeable](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17631) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint introduces Publication Authority as an exact-state, non-transferable, single-use publication capability and instantiates it in PAC-2026 (Publication-Accountability Calculus), a machine-readable AIJIM Protocol candidate. The paper evaluates its fourth bounded semantic freeze (SF-4), governed by six obligations covering evidence, runs and artifacts, measurement disclosure, authorization, surface correspondence, and lifecycle continuity. As AI-assisted claims increasingly appear authoritative, this protocol aims to ensure that evidence, analysis, human authorization, presentation, and correction history all refer to the same state, making such claims independently challengeable. It could influence how AI-generated research, journalism, and policy outputs are published and audited, though its practical adoption remains unproven. The evaluation explored 110,764 safe reachable states across ten models, with 76 unsafe configurations producing expected violations or observer countermodels; a historical predecessor path reproduced 17 frozen authorization-successor outcomes, and a later in-house test matched all 183 scored expectations. The authors explicitly state that results support internal coherence, bounded safety, fault sensitivity, and limited constructibility, but not factual truth, general refinement, blind interoperability, field efficacy, or standards status.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: AI-assisted claims often combine machine-generated evidence, analysis, and human sign-off, but these components can refer to different versions or states, creating an illusion of authority. Provenance, attestation, and transparency tools expose history but do not by themselves specify the exact publication transition being examined. PAC-2026 is a proposed machine-readable protocol that binds these elements into a single accountable publication event, using a semantic freeze (SF-4) to fix a specification profile for replaceable bindings.

## [SSLD Uses SDP Preprocessing to Improve DSATUR Graph Coloring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17633) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose SSLD (Semidefinite Spectral Learning with DSATUR), which preprocesses a single color class obtained from a semidefinite program before letting DSATUR color the rest of the graph. Evaluated on over 1600 benchmark instances including DIMACS, random graphs, Frequency Assignment, and Job Shop Scheduling, SSLD matches or beats DSATUR in almost every case and outperforms a naive GISD baseline. This is the first approach to improve DSATUR by preprocessing through fixed color classes, showing that SDP-guided preprocessing is a promising direction for future graph coloring heuristics. It could benefit applications such as register allocation, scheduling, and frequency assignment where fast heuristic coloring is used. SSLD achieves its quality at a runtime cost roughly 195 times slower than DSATUR, and the SDP is similar to the one used to compute the Lovász theta number. The evaluation covers DIMACS instances, Erdős–Rényi, Watts–Strogatz, and Barabási–Albert random graphs, plus Frequency Assignment and Job Shop Scheduling instances.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: The Graph Coloring Problem (GCP) asks for the minimum number of colors needed to color a graph so that no two adjacent vertices share a color, and it is NP-hard. DSATUR is a fast greedy heuristic introduced by Daniel Brélaz in 1979 that colors the vertex with the highest saturation degree first, but it typically uses more colors than state-of-the-art algorithms. Semidefinite programming (SDP) relaxations, such as the one behind the Lovász theta number, provide polynomial-time bounds on the chromatic number and can guide coloring heuristics.

## [GVD Framework Unifies Document Versioning and Deduplication](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17696) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers present GVD (Governed Versioning and Deduplication), a framework that unifies cross-document version linking with rule-level conflict resolution under an auditable update policy. On 120 enterprise documents processed as 140 ingestions across 59 version families, GVD achieves an F1 of 0.97 for version-family construction and 0.94 for rule-level consistency, with Counterfactual Span Probing (CSP) raising rule consistency from 0.90 to 0.94. Existing work treats versioning, duplicate detection, and contradiction detection as isolated pairwise tasks, so inconsistencies that belong to the growing collection rather than any single document are missed. GVD's unified, auditable approach could help organizations manage evolving policy and guideline repositories by suppressing duplicates and escalating only consequential changes for review. The pipeline runs fully locally with no large language model, assigning incoming documents to version families through bidirectional rule alignment and comparing rules against family memory to identify duplicates, contradictions, asymmetric refinements, and new knowledge. Counterfactual Span Probing resolves related pairs that inference misclassifies as neutral, and version lineage is retained as an audit trail.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Document repositories evolve continuously as guidelines and policies are revised, superseded, and re-uploaded, causing the same content to recur in different wording while newer versions refine or contradict earlier ones. Traditional approaches handle versioning, duplicate detection, and contradiction detection separately as pairwise tasks and stop once a pair is labeled, leaving collection-level inconsistencies unaddressed. GVD addresses this gap by treating these problems jointly under a governed update policy.

## [FairCompressAgent Uses LLM Planner for Fairness-Aware FPGA Model Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17786) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose FairCompressAgent (FCA), an agentic framework that combines fairness-aware pruning, incremental quantization, and sparse low-rank factorization behind a common operator interface, with a language-model planner selecting compression configurations based on model profiles and measured outcomes. On Fitzpatrick-17k with VGG-11, FCA selected a model with 59.54% less inference tensor storage while validation average precision rose from 0.5141 to 0.5233 and equalized opportunity (EOpp) fell from 0.2251 to 0.2168. Model compression is usually optimized only for accuracy and latency, so fairness can silently degrade when models are deployed on resource-constrained hardware such as FPGAs. By making fairness an explicit constraint in the compression search and supporting interactive requirement updates, FCA points toward deployment pipelines where accuracy, fairness, and cost are jointly managed rather than traded off blindly. FCA reaches the same final selection as one-shot planning while using 7.33 versus 12 candidate evaluations on average under their respective stopping policies, and it reports the remaining constraint violation when a user request cannot be satisfied. The evaluation covers 40 measured configurations and four search methods, with repeated fine-tuning, held-out testing, and online requirement updates used to characterize stability and interactive use.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Model compression reduces the size and compute cost of neural networks through techniques such as pruning, quantization, and low-rank factorization, which is important for deploying models on FPGAs and other edge hardware. Fairness-aware machine learning aims to ensure that model performance does not systematically disadvantage particular demographic groups, often measured by metrics such as equalized opportunity. The Fitzpatrick-17k dataset contains roughly 16,577 clinical skin images labeled with both diagnosis and Fitzpatrick skin type, making it a common benchmark for studying fairness in medical imaging. Agentic AI refers to systems in which a language model plans and selects actions, here choosing compression configurations based on measured feedback.

## [Masked-Concept Benchmark Finds Sparse TF-IDF Best for SNOMED CT Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17855) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces a masked-concept recommendation benchmark built on the SNOMED CT Entity Linking Challenge v1.2.1 data derived from MIMIC-IV-Note, containing 75,491 annotations across 272 discharge summaries (204 for training, 68 for testing). It compares a popularity baseline, sparse TF-IDF concept prototypes, dense LSA embeddings, sparse-dense fusion, retrieved-note evidence, and a retrieval-augmented hybrid, finding that sparse TF-IDF wins with Recall@1 of 14.81%, Recall@10 of 33.43%, MRR of 0.2114, and nDCG@10 of 0.2297. The results show that in low-resource clinical settings, simple lexical matching can beat more complex dense and retrieval-augmented pipelines, which challenges assumptions that neural retrieval is always superior. This provides a reproducible baseline for future ontology-grounded and biomedical-encoder retrieval systems, and highlights terminology coverage and concept frequency as key bottlenecks for clinical concept normalization. Performance is strongly tied to concept frequency: Recall@10 is only 7.74% for concepts appearing in one or two training notes versus 43.90% for concepts appearing in more than ten, and 9.66% of test note-concept pairs involve concepts never seen during training. Retrieval augmentation did not help, yielding Recall@10 of 31.99% and MRR of 0.1937, below the sparse TF-IDF baseline.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: SNOMED CT is a comprehensive, multilingual clinical healthcare terminology used to encode clinical meanings in electronic health records and support interoperability and analytics. MIMIC-IV-Note is a deidentified collection of free-text clinical notes, including hundreds of thousands of discharge summaries from Beth Israel Deaconess Medical Center. TF-IDF is a classic information-retrieval weighting scheme that scores how important a term is to a document relative to a corpus, and it remains a strong baseline in text retrieval tasks.

## [OBC-Prune: Outcome-Based Calibration for Pruning Large Reasoning Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17890) ⭐️ 6.0/10

OBC-Prune introduces outcome-based calibration for pruning large reasoning models, constructing difficulty-matched pairs of correct and incorrect rollouts and estimating each reasoning sentence's causal importance through intervention-based analysis. These causal scores are converted into per-token weights that rescale calibration activations for one-shot pruning methods like SparseGPT, Wanda, and ALPS without modifying the underlying algorithms. This matters because large reasoning models generate long chain-of-thought traces that create significant inference overhead, and existing pruning methods treat all reasoning tokens uniformly, allowing erroneous computation patterns to survive pruning and degrade reasoning quality. By preserving causally important reasoning circuits rather than statistically salient activations, OBC-Prune offers a more effective pruning objective that could reduce inference cost while maintaining accuracy on reasoning benchmarks. Experiments on DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen 1.5B, 7B, and 14B models at 40% and 50% sparsity show consistent improvements over state-of-the-art calibration baselines across most model sizes and sparsity levels on MATH500, LiveCodeBench, and AIME 2025. The method is designed as a drop-in calibration enhancement for existing one-shot pruning algorithms, though results vary by model size and sparsity level.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Pruning is a model compression technique that removes redundant parameters to reduce memory and compute costs, and post-training pruning methods like SparseGPT and Wanda rely on calibration data—a small set of examples used to estimate parameter importance. Large reasoning models (LRMs) such as DeepSeek-R1 produce long chain-of-thought traces before answering, which improves accuracy on complex tasks but greatly increases inference overhead. Recent work calibrates pruning on the model's own rollouts, but treats all reasoning tokens uniformly, so weights behind erroneous reasoning survive as readily as those behind correct reasoning.

A new arXiv preprint (2609.17921v1) proposes treating memory hierarchy, cross-agent sharing, and consistency mechanisms as central design considerations for collaborative multi-agent vision-language model (VLM) systems. Rather than presenting a benchmark or empirical breakthrough, the paper offers a design framework for how shared visual context should be organized and reconciled across agents. As multi-agent VLM systems move from research prototypes toward real deployments, the lack of principled shared visual memory becomes a practical bottleneck for reliability and resource efficiency. This framing could influence how future agent teams are architected, especially in domains like distributed perception, video analysis, and GUI automation where agents inspect different image regions or frames. The paper argues that shared visual memory must preserve not only images or textual summaries but also the dependencies among observations, agent interpretations, and subsequent reasoning, so agents can recover missing visual context and reconcile conflicting interpretations as new evidence emerges. It is a positioning/design contribution with no visible community discussion or engagement signals yet, and the abstract does not report quantitative results.

## [Multimodal SDXL Framework Generates Culturally Faithful Ulos Motifs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17987) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper proposes a multimodal generative framework that fine-tunes Stable Diffusion XL v1.0 via LoRA and integrates a Multimodal Large Language Model (LLaMA 1.5-7B) to controllably generate culturally faithful Batak Ulos motifs. A five-level ablation study across three scenarios shows that combining text, image, and semantic map conditioning via ControlNet achieved the best FID (270) and CLIP Score (0.65–0.70), while adding all four mechanisms worsened FID to 330 due to conflicting optimization signals. This work demonstrates how diffusion models and multimodal conditioning can be applied to cultural heritage preservation, offering a practical digital design tool for traditional weaving industries facing declining motif innovation. It also provides evidence that more conditioning mechanisms do not always yield better results, which is valuable for researchers designing controllable generation pipelines. The framework uses four complementary conditioning mechanisms—text, image, representation, and semantic map (via ControlNet)—each governing a distinct aspect from semantic intent to spatial layout. Text + Image + Representation offered the best overall balance with stable SSIM (0.84) and competitive FID (280), and qualitative evaluation by nine weavers and thirty public participants confirmed statistically significant positive acceptance (Wilcoxon, p=0.007 and p<0.001).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Stable Diffusion XL is a latent diffusion model that generates images from text prompts, and LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that adapts large models to specific domains with few trainable parameters. ControlNet is an add-on that lets users condition diffusion generation on spatial inputs such as segmentation maps, depth maps, or scribbles, enabling finer control over layout. Ulos is a traditional handwoven textile of the Batak people in Indonesia, and its motifs carry cultural significance that makes faithful generation challenging.

## [AALT: Composition-Aware Active Imitation Learning via Latent Topologies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18004) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Adaptive Agents via Latent Topologies (AALT), an active imitation learning method that requests demonstrations maximizing expected gains in start-goal connectivity rather than information gain about the expert policy. In a simulated UR5e ordered-retrieval domain with 72 tasks, AALT achieved 72/72 (100%) success using only 3 demonstrations totaling 5 transitions beyond the initial dataset, while the strongest baseline averaged 88.6% success after 20 demonstrations using 98 transitions. This work addresses a key inefficiency in active imitation learning for structured multi-task domains, where the number of start-goal tasks grows combinatorially but solutions share reusable behaviors. By explicitly valuing composable behaviors, AALT could significantly reduce expert effort and data requirements in robotics and other domains with many related tasks. AALT organizes existing demonstrations into a topology of latent hub states connected by learned behaviors, identifies high-value bridge demonstrations that enable many tasks at once, and grounds each to an expert query. At inference, it plans through the topology and conditions a diffusion policy on each successive hub transition; the objective is formally tied to information gain about task reachability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Active imitation learning allows a learner to request specific demonstrations from an expert, reducing the effort needed compared to passive imitation learning. In multi-task settings, tasks often share reusable sub-behaviors, so a single well-chosen demonstration can help solve many tasks at once. Latent topology representations model the environment as a graph of hidden states connected by learned behaviors, enabling planning and generalization.

<details><summary>References</summary>

## [PIVOT: Dual-Level Learning Framework for Visually-Grounded Multimodal Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18057) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper proposes PIVOT, a dual-level learning framework for reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) in large vision-language models (LVLMs). It introduces a self-calibrated experience replay mechanism that selectively collects and replays visually-grounded historical trajectories as stable reference anchors, plus a vision-guided advantage allocation mechanism that assigns extra vision-aware advantages to tokens based on their local visual support and downstream reasoning impact. Standard on-policy RLVR discards valuable visually-grounded reasoning trajectories after a single update and applies uniform token advantage allocation, which prevents models from reinforcing critical perception or reasoning steps. PIVOT addresses this optimization bottleneck and could improve multimodal reasoning capabilities across diverse benchmarks, benefiting researchers working on LVLM post-training and reinforcement learning. PIVOT combines two mechanisms: self-calibrated experience replay for stable reference anchors and vision-guided advantage allocation for token-level vision-aware advantages. The abstract reports highly competitive performance across diverse benchmarks, but provides no specific numbers, model sizes, or comparisons against baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) is a post-training method that fine-tunes models using automatic, rule-based checkers instead of learned reward models or human raters, and has significantly improved reasoning in large language and vision-language models. Experience replay, a technique from deep reinforcement learning (notably used in DQN and AlphaGo), stores past experiences and reuses them during training to stabilize and improve learning. Advantage allocation refers to how reinforcement learning algorithms distribute advantage values across actions or tokens to guide policy updates.

## [ARC: A Hubs-Based Framework for Scaling Rural K-12 AI and Robotics Education](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18072) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces AI, Robotics, & Community (ARC), a hubs-based framework in which colleges train undergraduate mentors and host workshops for nearby K-12 teams, with mature school programs becoming secondary hubs. A trial deployment at one university created three rural robotics teams and produced mean Likert-scale gains of 2.00, 2.25, and 1.25 points in K-12 programming knowledge, resource access, and practice opportunities, while undergraduate mentor confidence rose by 1.00–1.29 points across four measures. Rural K-12 robotics and AI education is hard to scale because it lacks sustained local technical mentorship, and ARC offers a practical, self-reinforcing model that could extend mentorship geographically. If the simulated growth holds, such hubs could substantially expand access to AI and robotics learning for underserved schools and simultaneously build teaching capacity among undergraduates. A spatial Markov model simulated with Indiana as a testbed projects that under moderate conditions ARC reaches 74% of the state's 1,925 public K-12 schools and produces 992 robotics programs after 40 years, versus 161 under natural growth alone. The trial evidence is limited to one university and relies on self-reported Likert surveys, so the growth projections remain model-based rather than empirically validated at regional scale.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: FIRST Robotics Competition is an international high school robotics program in which teams design, build, and program industrial-sized robots; in 2024 it involved 3,468 teams across 28 countries, but it still requires local programming and robotics expertise that many rural schools lack. A hubs-based education framework organizes learning around central institutions that support surrounding schools, and Likert-scale surveys are a common way to measure changes in attitudes or self-reported knowledge on a five-point scale.

## [Time-Aligned Evolving Concept Graphs for Scientific Relation Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18163) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.18163) proposes a time-aligned evolving concept graph framework that treats dated papers as shared update events, reconstructing semantic and structural states from the same publication history at each prediction time. Holding architecture and training fixed, refreshing context alongside graph updates improves mean relation AUPRC by 16.6% over frozen context, and the full framework raises mean relation AUROC from 0.9290 to 0.9722 on a graph of 187,848 papers, 270,687 concepts, and 7.45 million co-occurrence links. Scientific relation forecasting aims to identify promising connections before they emerge, so better temporal modeling could help researchers, funders, and institutions spot emerging research directions earlier. The work also highlights a general lesson for temporal graph learning: keeping semantic context synchronized with structural updates matters more than many pipelines assume. The framework jointly models semantic and structural evolution through pair-level fusion, forecasting first co-occurrence, relation formation, and conditional relation type. The reported mean population-weighted AUPRC is 0.005778, a very small absolute value that reflects the extreme sparsity of relation forecasting, so the AUROC and relative AUPRC gains are the more informative metrics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Scientific relation forecasting is the task of predicting which concepts, authors, or papers will become connected in the future, typically by learning from a knowledge graph of concepts and their co-occurrence links. Temporal graph learning studies how to model graphs whose nodes and edges change over time, and a common failure mode is that semantic representations are computed from coarse historical snapshots while the graph structure evolves much faster. This paper addresses that misalignment by rebuilding both semantic and structural states from the same dated publication events at each prediction time.

A new arXiv paper (2609.18278) reviews robustness, Operational Design Domain (ODD), and explainability as the three main fields needed to build trust in AI and achieve regulatory compliance for railway applications. It argues that these domains are already well studied by non-railway actors, with methods ready to be adapted for rail, and that a system view within a safe MLOps environment is necessary to satisfy regulators, operators, and the public. AI is currently restricted to non-safety-critical railway applications because of strict standards and regulations, so clarifying how robustness, ODD, and explainability can be combined into a trustworthy system view could unlock AI adoption in mission-critical rail operations. This matters for regulators, operators, and vendors seeking to deploy AI in safety-critical domains worldwide. The paper grounds robustness in ISO 24029 (assessment of neural network robustness) and ODD in the recently published DIN DKE SPEC 99004, which defines a methodology and modular taxonomy for ODD in rail. It is a review/position paper rather than a novel technical method, and it stresses that trustworthy requirements must interact continuously within a safe MLOps environment.

## [RiskWorld: Risk-Aware World Model for Selective Trajectory Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18442) ⭐️ 6.0/10

RiskWorld introduces a risk-aware world modeling framework that fuses spatial risk fields and temporal actor context with bird's-eye-view (BEV) visual features, using flow-guided occupancy evolution to forecast future occupancy. It generates one forecast per planning step that is reused across trajectory candidates, and only replaces the current planned trajectory when predicted risk triggers intervention and an alternative satisfies component-wise constraints on risk and trajectory error. Safe automated driving requires anticipating evolving traffic risks and deciding when to revise a planned trajectory, so a framework that selectively intervenes rather than replanning every step could improve both safety and computational efficiency. RiskWorld reports the lowest collision rate at a 3-second evaluation horizon on nuScenes, suggesting risk-aware world models can meaningfully reduce collisions in long-horizon planning. RiskWorld runs at 11.5 FPS on a single NVIDIA RTX 4090 with 90.81M parameters, and achieves the second-best average L2 error among state-of-the-art baselines. Evaluation is open-loop on nuScenes using camera features, annotation-derived current and historical actor states, and dataset-provided map context, with ablations showing lower collision rates than a current-state rescoring baseline while forecast reuse keeps the marginal cost of evaluating extra candidates low.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: World models in autonomous driving learn to predict how a scene will evolve, often by forecasting future occupancy grids that indicate which regions will be occupied by objects. Bird's-eye-view (BEV) representations project multi-sensor data into a unified top-down spatial grid, making them a common backbone for perception and planning. Risk fields assign spatial costs based on collision danger, and flow-guided evolution transports occupancy and scene features forward in time, with signed residuals correcting the transported occupancy. Selective trajectory replacement means the planner keeps its current trajectory unless predicted risk justifies switching to an alternative.

## [Hyperbolic Embeddings for Differential Diagnosis on Biomedical Knowledge Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18481) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A preliminary study (arXiv:2609.18481) applies hyperbolic graph representation learning to Mendelian-disease differential diagnosis on a patient-integrated biomedical knowledge graph. Experiments show hyperbolic models match or beat Euclidean baselines at substantially lower embedding dimensions, both on isolated ontology subgraphs and on a patient-level link-prediction task that ranks candidate diseases. Biomedical knowledge graphs mix tree-like ontology hierarchies with cross-cutting associations, and this work suggests hyperbolic geometry can exploit that hierarchy for clinical reasoning while using far fewer dimensions. If validated, lower-dimensional hyperbolic embeddings could make diagnostic reasoning models cheaper to train and deploy in healthcare settings. The study is explicitly preliminary: it evaluates models on isolated ontology subgraphs and a single link-prediction ranking task, without extensive clinical validation or large-scale patient cohorts. Hyperbolic models' advantage is most pronounced in low dimensions, but the abstract does not report absolute accuracy numbers or comparisons against state-of-the-art Euclidean graph neural networks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge graphs represent entities (phenotypes, diseases, genes, proteins, patients) as nodes and their relationships as edges, and graph representation learning converts these nodes into dense vectors so machine learning models can reason over them. Hyperbolic space, such as the Poincaré ball model, has negative curvature and can embed tree-like hierarchies with much lower distortion and fewer dimensions than ordinary Euclidean space. Differential diagnosis is the clinical process of ranking which diseases could explain a patient's observed phenotypes, and Mendelian diseases are rare inherited disorders with well-documented phenotype-genotype links.

## [TriProbe: Diagnosing Task Separability Beyond Accuracy in XAI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18525) ⭐️ 6.0/10

TriProbe introduces a multi-level probing framework that decomposes multi-task problems into binary subtasks and applies three probes — a Foundational Probe on input spaces, a Latent Probe on learned features, and a Final Probe on classifier outputs — using Maximum Fisher's Discriminant Ratio (F1) as a principled separability metric. Experiments on the Roshambo sEMG benchmark show it can reveal hidden breakdowns and identify bottlenecks and affected task pairs. Most learning pipelines are evaluated only by downstream accuracy, which leaves unexplained why tasks succeed or fail; TriProbe offers a diagnostic lens that can guide data collection, validation, and architecture design in multi-task learning. This matters for XAI and MTL practitioners who need to debug shared representations rather than treat models as black boxes. The framework uses Maximum Fisher's Discriminant Ratio (F1) as its separability metric, which measures class overlap in the best-disjunct feature dimension, and it decomposes multi-task problems into binary subtasks to isolate affected task pairs. Current validation is limited to the Roshambo sEMG benchmark, so broader generalization across domains remains to be demonstrated.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-task learning (MTL) trains a single model to solve several related tasks simultaneously by sharing representations, which can improve generalization but also makes it hard to tell which task or feature is causing a failure. Probing methods in explainable AI inspect intermediate representations to understand what information a model encodes. Surface electromyography (sEMG) measures muscle electrical activity via skin-surface sensors and is commonly used in biomechanics and rehabilitation, making it a natural testbed for multi-task signal classification. Maximum Fisher's Discriminant Ratio is a classical separability measure derived from Fisher's linear discriminant, quantifying how well a feature separates classes relative to within-class variance.

## [Survey of 118 Students Maps Uneven GenAI Learning Benefits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18676) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.18676) surveyed 118 students across 12 AI-related courses and identified four distinct GenAI user clusters, ranging from high-use students reporting many benefits to light users reporting fewer benefits. Using multivariable regression, the study linked academic benefit to early reliance and academic task support, while negative impact was associated with early reliance and attitudinal change. The findings suggest that the benefits of GenAI in education are unevenly distributed, shaped by tool tier, instructor policy, and students' evaluation literacy, which means institutions need targeted policies rather than one-size-fits-all rules. This matters for educators and administrators designing AI course policies, as well as for students who may be disadvantaged by premium tool access or unclear guidance. The study found significant differences between free- and premium-version users, single- and multiple-tool users, and students under different instructor policies; notably, the association between early reliance and negative impact grew stronger as evaluation literacy increased. Perceived GenAI-enhanced learning reflected cognitive, performance, and self-efficacy benefits, while concerns about stress and diminished critical thinking correlated with lower perceived learning benefits.
