## [ME-Decoding Reframes LLM Token Selection as Ensemble Pruning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18723) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.18723v1) introduces Mahalanobis-Ensemble Decoding (ME-Decoding), a decoding framework that treats candidate token selection as an ensemble pruning problem. It uses a Mahalanobis distance-driven objective with a token similarity matrix built from an adaptive-bandwidth kernel over token embeddings, plus a greedy selection algorithm with near-linear complexity under early stopping and theoretical approximation guarantees. Most LLM decoding strategies rely on scalar probabilities alone, which can produce redundant candidates and limit semantic diversity; ME-Decoding offers a plug-and-play module with negligible inference overhead that could improve reasoning and generation quality without retraining. If adopted, it could influence how sampling and beam-search alternatives are designed across the NLP ecosystem. The method dynamically discounts redundant generation paths via a token similarity matrix constructed with an adaptive-bandwidth kernel over token embeddings, and the greedy selection algorithm achieves near-linear complexity in candidate size under early stopping while retaining approximation guarantees. The paper claims consistent gains across diverse reasoning and generation tasks, though it is positioned as an incremental research contribution rather than an immediate industrial breakthrough.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: LLM decoding is the process of turning a model's next-token probability distribution into actual text, typically via greedy search, beam search, top-k, or nucleus sampling. Ensemble pruning is a classical machine-learning technique that selects a smaller subset of models or candidates from a larger group to reduce redundancy and cost while preserving or improving performance. The Mahalanobis distance is a multivariate statistical metric that measures how far a point is from a distribution while accounting for correlations between variables, making it useful for detecting redundancy in high-dimensional embedding spaces.

Its evaluation reveals substantial disparities across capability dimensions, especially in affective interpretation and compositional reasoning, highlighting challenges for building trustworthy multimodal models for education. MUSE's decoupled annotation and question generation design enables diverse tasks with controllable difficulty while reducing annotation effort, and the benchmark deliberately centers Singaporean and Southeast Asian multicultural contexts alongside Western art traditions across multiple themes and difficulty levels. ## [REQAP: Fault-Tolerant Weight Packing and Quantization for Edge DNN Accelerators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17555) ⭐️ 6.0/10

REQAP introduces a sensitivity-driven mixed-precision quantization framework combined with deterministic register-level weight packing and selective bit replication for systolic-array-based edge DNN accelerators. Simulations on AlexNet, VGG-11, and ResNet-18 show up to 62% memory reduction and up to 56% fewer MAC operations, while improving accuracy resilience under fault injection compared to baseline and fully protected models. Edge AI deployment demands aggressive model compression while operating on hardware prone to faults, and REQAP addresses both concerns simultaneously by co-designing quantization and fault tolerance. If validated on real silicon, this approach could make embedded DNN inference more reliable and efficient for safety-critical applications such as autonomous systems and industrial IoT. The method enforces symmetric precision between weights and activations, packs heterogeneous operand pairs into fixed-width register words for SWAR-style parallel execution, and replicates the most significant bits (MSBs) of critical layers into unused register space to achieve TMR-style protection with minimal overhead. Evaluation relies on a custom systolic-array simulation framework rather than fabricated hardware, so real-world area, power, and timing costs remain to be confirmed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Systolic arrays are a common architecture for DNN accelerators, where processing elements pass data rhythmically to perform matrix multiplication efficiently. Mixed-precision quantization assigns different bit-widths to different layers to balance accuracy and efficiency, while SWAR (SIMD Within A Register) packs multiple small operands into one register for parallel processing. Hardware faults in such accelerators can degrade inference accuracy, motivating fault-tolerance techniques like triple modular redundancy (TMR), which normally incurs large overhead.

## [WARD: Runtime-Adaptive Vision Transformer Framework for Dependable Edge AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17556) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers present WARD, a runtime-adaptive Vision Transformer framework that combines channel-wise subnetwork partitioning, reliability-aware continual learning, and dynamic operating-mode scheduling. Implemented on a lightweight FPGA-based accelerator, WARD achieves a network-level failure rate of only 1.79% under high Bit Error Rates, with under 5% area overhead and mode transitions within a few clock cycles. Edge AI systems such as autonomous vehicles, industrial monitoring, and satellite onboard intelligence must operate under changing power budgets, reliability requirements, and input distributions, yet existing fault-tolerant methods assume static conditions. WARD shows that adaptive reliability management can be integrated into programmable edge AI accelerators with negligible overhead, potentially enabling more dependable real-time inference in long-running deployments. WARD uses two physically isolated subnetworks that execute under four operating modes: Full-Precision Mode, Low-Power Mode, High-Reliability Mode, and Adaptive Mode, dynamically adjusting computational cost and reliability while ensuring uninterrupted inference. The FPGA-based implementation supports runtime mode transitions within a few clock cycles and incurs less than 5% area overhead, though the work is a preprint without peer review or community discussion yet.

**Background**: Vision Transformers (ViTs) adapt the Transformer architecture, originally designed for natural language processing, to computer vision tasks by processing images as sequences of patches. Continual learning refers to a neural network's ability to acquire knowledge from a stream of non-stationary data while preserving earlier competencies. Edge AI deployments often rely on programmable accelerators like FPGAs, where hardware faults such as bit errors can corrupt inference, making fault tolerance and runtime adaptation critical.

## [BLADE: Reliability-Aware Boundary Selection for Hybrid SNN-ANN Event Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17562) ⭐️ 6.0/10

BLADE is presented as the first reliability-aware methodology for jointly selecting the SNN-ANN boundary and ANN early-exit configuration in dynamic hybrid networks, using hierarchical statistical fault injection during design-space exploration. On an event-based object detector it achieves mAP 0.5 of 0.691 while cutting inference compute energy to 15.82 mJ when the ANN early exit fires. Existing hybrid SNN-ANN designs pick the boundary using only accuracy and energy, ignoring reliability and dynamic inference, which is risky for safety-critical edge AI. By making reliability a first-class optimization objective, BLADE points toward more dependable deployment of neuromorphic hybrid systems in applications such as autonomous drones and smart surveillance. Reliability analysis identifies the most significant floating-point exponent bit as the dominant source of catastrophic failures, causing significant-or-worse accuracy degradation in 58.8% of its fault injections; protecting this single bit costs about 3% storage overhead and eliminates catastrophic failures at the evaluated realistic fault rates. Increasing the SNN share also improves fault tolerance, with the fully SNN configuration retaining a reliability of 0.965 under aggressive fault conditions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Spiking neural networks (SNNs) process information as discrete spikes and are prized for energy efficiency, but they generally trail conventional artificial neural networks (ANNs) in detection accuracy. Hybrid SNN-ANN architectures aim to get the best of both worlds by using early SNN layers to capture temporal event information and ANN layers for accurate, GPU-friendly detection. Event cameras output asynchronous per-pixel brightness-change events rather than frames, making them attractive for low-power edge vision but also making the design space of such hybrid pipelines complex.

## [Polynomial Realisation of Independence Systems in Unsplittable Flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17568) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.17568) introduces a path-closed notion of realising an independence system via zero-cost choices of primary terminals in a directed acyclic flow instance, and proves that every finite loopless independence system admits a polynomial-size realisation measured in the incidence size of its minimal forbidden sets. The construction is specialised to odd cycles C_{2k+1}, yielding a uniform rational family whose fractional cheap-selection vector violates the odd-cycle inequality, with an exact cost-preserving additive-congestion threshold tau = 1 - bq and a supremum threshold of (k+2)/(2(k+1)) within the symmetric family. This result generalises the triangle mechanism for realising independence systems in single-source unsplittable flow, showing that every finite simple graph and every hypergraph independence system without singleton forbidden hyperedges can be represented by an acyclic single-source gadget. It strengthens the theoretical toolkit for combinatorial optimisation and algorithmic graph theory, with potential implications for understanding integrality gaps and congestion thresholds in unsplittable routing. The realisation definition quantifies over every directed source-terminal path, making it robust under prefix borrowing, suffix splicing, and hybrid routes; for C5 the authors provide an exact certificate that independently derives all source-terminal paths and enumerates all 3^10 = 59049 unsplittable routings using rational arithmetic. The supremum threshold (k+2)/(2(k+1)) tends to 1/2 as k grows, and the construction relies on a potential shift converting a signed connector separator into nonnegative arc costs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: An independence system is a family of subsets of a ground set that is closed under taking subsets, generalising matroids and capturing feasible sets in combinatorial optimisation. In the single-source unsplittable flow problem, a common source must route each terminal's demand along a single path without splitting it, and additive-congestion constraints can enforce stable-set structure. Directed acyclic graphs (DAGs) are directed graphs with no directed cycles, and they are used here as the underlying flow instances because they permit clean path-based definitions and avoid cyclic dependencies.

## [EES: Council-Guided Program Evolution with Persistent Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17590) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.17590v1) introduces Evolutionary Ensemble Search (EES), which builds machine-learning procedures through expert-guided program evolution. A role-specialized council converts task evidence and experimental results into structured search directions, which an orchestrator dispatches to execution specialists and an evolutionary engine that mutates, edits, and crosses over code. On a public MLE-bench Lite development ledger, EES recorded medal-threshold artifacts on 19 of 22 tasks (86.36%), with 11 gold, five silver, and three bronze outcomes. EES proposes a concrete architecture for cumulative, executable search in which every candidate program must run and acquire its own validation evidence, which could inform AutoML and evolutionary computation research on how to combine LLM-style expert guidance with program synthesis. Its cross-modal results across text, images, tables, audio, and scientific geometry suggest that such guided evolutionary search may generalize beyond single-domain benchmarks, though the reported numbers are development results rather than a blind autonomous-agent success rate. The evolutionary engine selects measured parents, diagnoses their errors, and produces descendants via code mutation, structured pipeline edits, and crossover, while population archives retain useful alternatives and compatible predictions compete in a validation-gated ensemble stage. Search adapts through parent-relative operator credit, session memory, and problem-indexed lessons retrieved across runs, and the paper explicitly defines the contracts needed to compare candidates as their computations change; the campaign also includes grade feedback between runs and external-source routes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Evolutionary computation solves problems by iteratively mutating and recombining candidate solutions, and ensemble learning combines multiple models to improve robustness. AutoML aims to automate the design of machine-learning pipelines, while program synthesis generates code automatically from specifications. EES sits at the intersection of these areas: it treats machine-learning procedures as evolvable programs, uses a council of specialized roles to steer the search, and keeps persistent memory so lessons from earlier runs can be reused. MLE-bench is a benchmark of machine-learning engineering tasks where performance is measured against medal thresholds similar to Kaggle competitions.

## [High-gain gated-FFN rows: structural prominence is not causal importance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17599) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2609.17599) reports a frozen 22-model causal census of high-gain rows in gated feed-forward networks across both text and genomic foundation models, computing the associated bilinear weight operator exactly rather than via diagonal approximation. The study finds that activation-derived high-gain candidates are functionally enriched over random and top-norm controls, but neither spectral concentration nor operator magnitude predicts causal effect size, and these associations disappear within the endpoint-homogeneous text-decoder subset. This is a valuable negative result for mechanistic interpretability: it warns researchers against treating structural extremeness (e.g., unusually large weights or high-gain rows) as a transferable proxy for functional criticality. It also suggests that interpretability findings may not transfer cleanly between text and genomic foundation models, since the causal organization of critical rows appears model-specific even when enrichment is general. A within-layer sweep of 36 rows in one genomic and one text decoder revealed two regimes: below the detector's acceptance threshold the ratio carried no positive information about causal damage, while above it the ratio strongly ordered rows but did not grade severity as a dose-response. The same sweep uncovered a second individually catastrophic row invisible to a one-candidate-per-model census, non-additive damage among co-located critical rows, a robust super-additive pair interaction in DNABERT-2, and in GENERator a sharply position-localized dependence where restoring the row's beginning-of-sequence contribution rescued essentially all native-loss damage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Transformer language models contain gated feed-forward networks (FFNs), where a small number of unusually high-gain parameters can exert disproportionate effects on model behavior. Mechanistic interpretability aims to identify which internal components are causally important, often by looking for structural outliers such as high-norm or high-gain rows. Genomic foundation models (e.g., DNABERT-2, GENERator) apply similar transformer architectures to DNA sequences, raising the question of whether the same structural signatures mark critical components across domains.

## [Decentralized Algorithm Learns Socially Optimal Equilibria Over Dynamic Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17601) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.17601) proposes networked decentralized optimal equilibrium learning dynamics in which agents in a finite normal-form game exchange time-stamped, time-stacked tables built from randomized semantic content/discontent signals rather than raw actions or payoff information. The method combines table fusion with temporal majority reconstruction and is proven to achieve finite-time logarithmic regret for optimal equilibrium selection under both utilitarian and proportional-fair social welfare objectives. Equilibrium selection in multi-agent systems usually requires either a central coordinator or heavy information sharing, so a scheme that reaches socially desirable equilibria using only low-bandwidth local signals could make decentralized coordination practical in settings like networked markets, robot swarms, and distributed resource allocation. The finite-time logarithmic regret guarantee also gives a theoretical performance bound that most heuristic decentralized learning schemes lack. Each agent observes only its own realized payoffs and does not know the game a priori, communicating solely with time-varying neighbors; the algorithm adds an in-phase exploration perturbation to support the regret analysis. The regret guarantees are established for utilitarian and proportional-fair social welfare objectives, and simulation results show effective selection of socially desirable equilibria over dynamic communication networks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: In game theory, a finite normal-form game describes interactions among agents with a fixed set of strategies, and an equilibrium is a strategy profile from which no agent wants to unilaterally deviate; however, many games have multiple equilibria, so choosing the one that maximizes collective welfare is a separate problem known as equilibrium selection. Decentralized learning studies how agents can converge to such outcomes without a central controller, and regret measures the cumulative welfare loss relative to the best possible outcome, with logarithmic regret indicating that losses grow very slowly over time. Social welfare objectives such as utilitarian welfare (sum of utilities) and proportional fairness provide different criteria for judging which equilibrium is socially desirable.

A new arXiv paper (2609.17644) introduces a curated astronomy QA benchmark of 300 free-response questions drawn from 2017–2026 Olympiad-style materials, including 204 text-only and 96 image-linked items. Comparing open-weight and API-served general-purpose, multimodal, and astronomy-specialized models with judge-based correctness and reference metrics, the study finds strong general-purpose models achieve the highest correctness baseline. The results challenge the common assumption that domain-specific fine-tuning is always superior for scientific QA, suggesting that specialization should be viewed as a task- and deployment-dependent property. This has practical implications for researchers and organizations deciding whether to invest in specialized models or rely on general-purpose systems for scientific workflows. The benchmark's free-response subset includes 96 image-linked questions, and the analysis examines metric agreement, judge sensitivity, benchmark composition, and modality effects that a single leaderboard would not capture. The authors note future work will extend the benchmark across task formats, adaptation strategies, and scientific reliability measures, with a reserved multiple-choice track for comparing constrained-answer accuracy against open-ended reasoning.

## [QiT: Quantum-Inspired Transformer for Visual Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17789) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduce QiT, a quantum-inspired Transformer for vision tasks that uses angle-inspired encoding, periodic self-attention, and gated multiplicative emulation to bring quantum representational ideas into a classical model. QiT-B achieves 78.3% ImageNet-1K top-1 accuracy with 45.7M parameters and 11.5 GFLOPs, matching a classical Transformer while avoiding the runtime cost of a small simulated quantum Transformer. This work offers a scalable way to isolate and evaluate quantum-motivated inductive biases in classical vision models without requiring quantum hardware, which could guide future architecture design and quantum machine learning research. It also provides a practical baseline for comparing quantum-inspired ideas against standard Vision Transformers. QiT retains the O(N^2D) attention complexity of a standard Vision Transformer and claims neither quantum computation nor quantum speedup; all components are differentiable tensor operations. The model uses a classical cosine kernel approximated to quantum fidelity kernels and a trainable classical surrogate for variational circuit interaction terms.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Quantum machine learning often uses angle encoding, where classical data is embedded into quantum states via rotation gates, creating periodic similarities in Hilbert space. However, current quantum neural networks are limited by qubit counts, noise, and costly simulation, making them impractical for large-scale vision tasks. QiT instead translates these quantum structural ideas into classical Transformer operations, aiming to capture useful inductive biases without quantum hardware.

## [Reinforcement Learning Adapts FOM-ROM Coupling in Schwarz Domain Decomposition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.17837) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces a reinforcement learning approach that uses deep Q-networks (DQNs) to adaptively select between full-order models (FOMs) and operator inference (OpInf) reduced-order models (ROMs) within the overlapping Schwarz alternating method (O-SAM) for transient problems. The DQNs are trained offline with a reward balancing accuracy, cost, and model-switching frequency, then deployed predictively on unseen problem instances without requiring a reference FOM solution. This work addresses a key limitation of hybrid domain decomposition methods, which typically keep the model assigned to each subdomain fixed throughout a simulation, making them inefficient for transient problems where localized features propagate and high-fidelity regions must move over time. By enabling online, predictive adaptation of model fidelity, the approach could improve the efficiency of multiscale simulations in scientific computing and engineering. The approach is demonstrated on two examples: a 1D advection-diffusion problem with a moving front, where the learned policy dynamically allocates high-fidelity resolution as the front propagates and outperforms static FOM/ROM assignments, and a 3D linear elastic wave propagation problem implemented in the Norma.jl solid mechanics code. For the advection-diffusion benchmark, letting the agent also adapt the domain decomposition provided no further benefit, and for the elastic wave benchmark, learned policies for two and three subdomain decompositions tracked the propagating wave by assigning FOMs to subdomains containing the wave and ROMs elsewhere.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Hybrid domain decomposition methods couple full-order models (FOMs), which solve the governing equations at high fidelity but are expensive, with reduced-order models (ROMs), which are cheap but approximate. Operator inference (OpInf) is a nonintrusive, data-driven technique that learns reduced model operators from snapshot data while preserving the algebraic structure of the governing equations. The overlapping Schwarz alternating method (O-SAM), introduced by Hermann Schwarz in 1869–1870, is an iterative domain decomposition method that solves subdomain-local problems while exchanging solution information through transmission boundary conditions on overlapping interfaces. Deep Q-networks (DQNs) are a reinforcement learning technique that combines Q-learning with deep neural networks, using experience replay and target networks to learn policies from high-dimensional state spaces.

## [Lightweight CNN-Transformer Hybrid Achieves 99-100% Breast Cancer Mammography Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18212) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.18212) presents a lightweight hybrid model that integrates a Compact Convolutional Transformer (CCT) after CNN layers, using a CCT tokenizer to reshape CNN features into compact patch tokens with positional embeddings. Tested via 5-fold cross-validation on three breast cancer mammography datasets, the model achieved 99%-100% accuracy with only 250,435 parameters, and integrated Explainable AI (XAI) to make its classification decisions interpretable. The extremely small parameter count makes the model suitable for computer-aided diagnosis in resource-constrained clinical environments, where large Vision Transformers are impractical. The integration of XAI also addresses the critical need for transparency and clinician trust in AI-driven mammography screening tools. The model overcomes the gradient degradation problem in the last CNN layers by combining convolutional tokenization with transformer-based learning, and it is lighter than ViT while still capturing long-range dependencies among breast tissue regions. However, the results come from only three datasets and would need broader external validation before clinical adoption.

## [CPR framework unifies continuous autoregressive piano rendering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18216) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces the Composer-Performer-Refiner (CPR) framework, which combines continuous autoregressive modeling with local flow matching and full-sequence refining for prompt-conditioned piano MIDI-to-Music rendering. It also proposes Bottlenecked Representation Alignment (BREPA) and Modality-Time RoPE (MT-RoPE) to improve musical semantics and cross-modal temporal alignment, with code released on GitHub. This work addresses a key trade-off in generative audio: discrete-codec autoregressive models lose acoustic detail through quantization, while flow matching and diffusion preserve acoustics but incur high full-sequence attention costs. By operating directly on continuous representations, CPR could enable higher-quality, more efficient piano rendering and inform future continuous autoregressive approaches in music and speech generation. The Composer autoregressively predicts continuous hidden states, the Performer generates 24kHz acoustic latents via local flow matching, and the Refiner upsamples the waveform to 48kHz. BREPA strengthens musical semantic structure in the Composer hidden states, while MT-RoPE improves temporal alignment across modalities.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Prompt-conditioned piano MIDI-to-Music rendering aims to faithfully render target notes while reproducing the timbre of a reference recording. Existing approaches mainly follow two paradigms: autoregressive modeling, which provides causal temporal modeling but suffers from quantization loss in discrete-codec variants, and flow matching or diffusion, which better preserves acoustic structure at the cost of full-sequence attention and weaker semantic structure. Continuous autoregressive models operate directly on continuous representations, combining the condition-following ability of autoregressive models with the distribution-modeling capacity of flow matching while avoiding quantization.

## [GPT-5 Scores Classroom Interactions in Hong Kong Kindergartens](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18274) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv study evaluated whether GPT-5 can score teacher-child interactions using the full Classroom Assessment Scoring System (CLASS) framework, analyzing 87 video-recorded observations from 38 classrooms across 30 Hong Kong kindergartens. AI-rated scores converged with human raters most strongly for the Emotional Support domain and the Quality of Feedback dimension, but diverged for procedural or context-dependent interactions in Classroom Organization and Instructional Support. Classroom observation is central to measuring education quality but is costly and dependent on trained human observers, so demonstrating that an LLM can partially replicate CLASS ratings suggests a path toward lower-cost, scalable screening. However, the study's finding that AI cannot yet reproduce calibrated human judgements across the full framework cautions against using such scores for high-stakes teacher evaluation. The AI model was configured to apply the full CLASS framework based only on observation transcripts, meaning visual and contextual classroom information was unavailable to it. The authors conclude that transcript-based AI scoring may capture relative variation in teacher-child interactions but is better suited as a preliminary screening and teacher reflection tool than as a replacement for trained observers.

Quality of Feedback, a dimension within Instructional Support, measures how teachers respond to children's efforts in ways that extend their learning and reasoning. GPT-5 is OpenAI's multimodal large language model released on August 7, 2025, succeeding GPT-4.

## [Trajectory Learnability for Offline On-Policy Distillation with Imperfect Teachers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18321) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces a method to determine what remains learnable from imperfect teacher supervision in offline on-policy distillation (OPD) by using teacher-successful problems as a reference to measure signed likelihood changes. It aggregates these changes into trajectory-level weights for the original distillation loss, improving an offline OPD baseline by up to 2.7 percentage points on mathematical reasoning and code generation while using only 2 GPUs and about 22 GPU hours. Offline OPD is attractive because it reuses stored student trajectories and teacher supervision, but the same reuse makes imperfect supervision persistent, so knowing which parts of a trajectory are still worth learning from directly affects distillation quality. This work offers a cheap, generation-free way to filter teacher supervision, potentially making offline OPD competitive with or better than online OPD at much lower compute cost. The learnability signal is computed once from stored trajectories and model checkpoints by training on teacher-successful problems and measuring signed likelihood changes of tokens in teacher-failed trajectories, so no additional generation is needed. The method uses 2 GPUs and about 22 GPU hours, compared with 3 GPUs and 36–48 GPU hours for representative online OPD methods, though it adds an extra successful-only distillation stage.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge distillation transfers knowledge from a large teacher model to a smaller student model, often by having the student match the teacher's output distribution rather than only the correct label. On-policy distillation (OPD) collects trajectories generated by the student and uses teacher supervision on those trajectories, and the offline variant collects this data once and reuses it throughout optimization. Because even strong teachers make mistakes, the reused supervision can be wrong, and this paper asks how to identify which parts of a teacher-failed trajectory are still learnable.

## [GYROval: A Robust Benchmark for Cultural Value Orientation in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18384) ⭐️ 6.0/10

GYROval introduces a publicly released benchmark that measures the cultural value orientation of large language models along the two Inglehart-Welzel axes across multiple domains, roles, languages, and sampling temperatures, and reports results for twenty models. Eleven of those models were additionally tested with a paired Russian translation of the identical items and a second sampling temperature, with stability assessed via tie-corrected Kendall's W against an empirical permutation null. As LLMs are deployed globally, understanding whether their value orientations shift across languages, roles, and sampling settings is important for culturally aware alignment and evaluation. GYROval's stability analysis and public release offer a reusable instrument for researchers studying cultural bias and consistency in model behavior. The benchmark uses binary contrastive scenarios in the style of CDEval, where both options are legitimate and there is no answer key, so a model's axis score is simply the proportion of responses falling on the counted pole. Stability is measured by treating each vignette as the unit of analysis, ranking models within perturbation levels, and comparing agreement across levels with tie-corrected Kendall's W.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: The Inglehart-Welzel cultural map, developed by political scientists Ronald Inglehart and Christian Welzel, arranges societies along two axes: Traditional vs. Secular-Rational Values and Survival vs. Self-expression Values, based on the World Values Survey. CDEval, introduced in 2023, was an earlier benchmark for measuring the cultural dimensions of LLMs using contrastive scenarios. GYROval builds on this line of work by adding robustness checks across languages and temperatures.

## [Non-Linear Neuron Detects Isolated Pixels Without Thresholds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18399) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.18399) proposes a non-linear neuron-based method for detecting isolated pixels in both binary and grayscale images using spatially diameter-limited receptive fields with excitatory and inhibitory regions. The approach modifies a neuron model originally designed for anomaly detection and eliminates the need for user-specified thresholds or parameters. Isolated pixel detection is important in medical imaging, astronomy, quality control, and even cybersecurity tasks framed as image processing, so a threshold-free and noise-robust method could simplify workflows and improve reliability over existing template matching and second-order derivative techniques. It may also be useful for identifying anomalous single nodes in spatially organized networks. The method operates on spatially diameter-limited receptive fields that combine excitatory and inhibitory regions, making it applicable to both binary and grayscale images. The abstract notes that smoothing the image before processing improved detection in X-ray imagery, and the approach is described as effective, robust, and efficient without user-specified parameters.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Detecting isolated points is a classic image processing task, often handled by template matching or second-order derivative operators such as the Laplacian. Template matching becomes infeasible for grayscale images, while derivative-based methods are highly sensitive to noise and require manually chosen thresholds. Receptive fields are inspired by biological neurons, where excitatory and inhibitory regions respond to contrast in a local neighborhood.

## [TERN: Delta-Rule Memory with Seasonal Reference for Influenza Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18407) ⭐️ 6.0/10

TERN introduces a delta-rule fast-weight memory that decays channel-wise and erases along a learned address under gates driven by local epidemic-phase features, combined with an explicit seasonal reference and online adaptation. On three Cola-GNN influenza benchmarks, TERN outperformed epidemic graph models and general forecasters, matched or exceeded seasonal references, and a controlled comparison confirmed the contribution of the memory itself. Weekly influenza surveillance counts guide vaccine distribution and public-health alerts, yet they are hard to forecast because each region offers only a few seasons and wave timing and height shift yearly. TERN's phase-aware memory could improve regional epidemic forecasting, though its impact is likely limited to specialized time-series forecasting rather than broad AI/ML. The memory decays channel-wise and erases along a learned address under gates driven by local epidemic-phase features, addressing the problem that information helpful during a growing wave misleads after its peak. A controlled comparison confirmed the memory's contribution, but the approach is evaluated only on influenza benchmarks, so generalization to other diseases remains untested.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: The delta rule is a gradient descent learning rule for updating weights in a single-layer neural network, and it underlies fast-weight programmers, where a slow controller network dynamically modifies the fast weights of a memory via differentiable update rules. Cola-GNN is a cross-location attention-based graph neural network for long-term influenza-like-illness prediction, and its influenza datasets are widely used as benchmarks. Epidemic forecasting is challenging because each region has few seasons of data, waves shift in timing and height, and information that helps during a growing wave can mislead after the peak.

## [Delphos uses multitask RL to automate discrete choice model specification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18441) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers present Delphos, a multitask reinforcement learning framework that learns transferable specification strategies across transport choice datasets. Trained on nine datasets, it outperforms single-task agents and, without further training, matches or beats expert and metaheuristic specifications on the unseen Swissmetro and Decisions datasets in under 20 minutes on a standard CPU. Discrete choice model specification is traditionally a slow, manual trial-and-error process, so automating it could significantly reduce modeller effort while preserving human control over final model selection. The demonstrated transfer across datasets suggests that accumulated modelling experience can be reused, potentially making econometric modelling more efficient in transportation research and beyond. Delphos frames specification as a sequential decision-making problem and represents utility specifications as sets of modelling terms using a DeepSet-Q architecture, enabling a shared policy to handle datasets with different variable sets. It achieves higher log-likelihood per observation than the VNS metaheuristic on Swissmetro and performance comparable to a published MNL specification by expert modellers on Decisions, though the paper does not claim full autonomy over diagnosis and refinement.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Discrete choice models are widely used in transportation and economics to explain how people choose among alternatives such as travel modes, and their utility specifications must balance goodness-of-fit, parsimony, and behavioural plausibility. Determining these specifications is time-consuming and error-prone, which has motivated prior work on assisted specification using multi-objective and combinatorial optimization. Multitask reinforcement learning trains a single policy across related tasks to improve sample efficiency and knowledge transfer, and Delphos applies this idea to model specification.

## [CSWAM adds causal semantic expert to FastWAM for better OOD generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18462) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces CSWAM (Causal Semantic World Action Model), which augments the FastWAM world action model with a causal semantic expert built on V-JEPA 2.1. On RoboTwin 2.0 Clean-to-Randomized transfer, CSWAM raises success from 10.16% to 45.18% (a 35.02-point gain over FastWAM), and across two real-robot tasks and three OOD difficulty levels it improves average success from 27.5% to 70.0% (a 42.5-point gain). World action models are a promising alternative to vision-language-action models for embodied control, but they degrade sharply under visual distribution shifts, which limits real-world deployment. CSWAM's large gains on both simulated and real-robot OOD benchmarks suggest that injecting temporally grounded semantic representations can substantially improve robustness without sacrificing FastWAM's efficient action-only inference. CSWAM's causal semantic expert learns the future evolution of semantic state changes and motion from a sparse history of current and past observations, sharing history-derived context with both the video and action streams via causal attention. At inference, the model conditions action denoising on the current video state and observed semantic history, retaining efficient action-only inference; the reported gains depend on embodied pretraining.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: World Action Models (WAMs) explicitly model how visual observations evolve under actions and have emerged as an alternative to Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models for embodied control. FastWAM is a WAM variant that keeps video co-training during training but skips future prediction at test time, enabling efficient action-only inference, yet its reconstruction-oriented representations emphasize appearance-specific details and generalize poorly to unseen scenes and objects. V-JEPA 2.1 is a family of self-supervised video models from Meta FAIR that learns dense spatio-temporal representations with less dependence on appearance, making it well suited to supply semantic state-change and motion features. Out-of-distribution (OOD) generalization refers to maintaining performance when test conditions differ from training, a key challenge for robot policies.

## [CoRe-MARL Uses Recurrent MARL for Emergency Supply Redistribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18639) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces CoRe-MARL, a cooperative multi-agent reinforcement learning framework that formulates emergency supply redistribution among decentralized centers as a Dec-POMDP. It combines recurrent networks with multi-agent proximal policy optimization (MAPPO) for centralized training and decentralized execution, and shows in simulation that MAPPO reduces the service gap across centers and improves the worst-served region compared with recurrent IPPO and a local-only heuristic. This work shows how cooperative MARL can improve equitable service delivery in decentralized emergency management, where local centers make independent decisions under uncertain demand and disrupted transportation. It offers a practical template for applying Dec-POMDP and CTDE methods to real-world supply chain and humanitarian logistics problems. The framework assumes actors and the MAPPO critic do not observe exact dynamics, relying on recurrent networks to capture evolving supply and demand from partial observations. Evaluation uses simulated trajectories with diverse patterns, and MAPPO maintains competitive network-wide service while improving the worst-served center, though results are simulation-based rather than field-deployed.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: A Dec-POMDP is a formal framework for cooperative sequential decision making by a team of agents, each with only local, partial observations of the system state. Multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) studies how multiple agents learn policies in shared environments, and centralized training with decentralized execution (CTDE) lets agents use global information during training while acting only on local observations at deployment. MAPPO is a popular CTDE algorithm that extends proximal policy optimization to multi-agent settings.

A new arXiv preprint (2609.18739) proposes a multi-step framework that automatically improves the annotation quality of Named Entity Recognition (NER) datasets. A scalable, automated correction method could substantially reduce manual re-annotation costs and improve downstream NLP systems for underrepresented languages. ## [StableEval Arena: A Cost-Aware Benchmark for Stablecoin Peg-Risk Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18949) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced StableEval Arena, a cost-aware benchmark that evaluates LLM-backed agentic systems on diagnosing stablecoin peg stress and forecasting deviations from the one-dollar peg over a hidden seven-day horizon. The framework reports results across two experiment blocks — a 120-case stress-enriched validation set and a 507-case natural-distribution full-arena evaluation — covering six LLM-backed agent configurations and baselines, and releases the dataset on Hugging Face and the source code on GitHub. As stablecoins grow into a core piece of crypto market infrastructure, reliable early warning of peg stress matters for traders, issuers, and regulators, and this benchmark shifts agentic evaluation away from pure accuracy toward trustworthiness as a joint property of forecast quality, operational reliability, and computational cost. It also exposes a concrete gap: agents that reliably follow output protocols still miss most rare severe-stress and sustained-depeg cases, which is exactly the scenario where such systems would be most valuable. Evaluation uses leakage-safe historical replay with exchange price-volume data and market-context features, and measures prediction quality, calibrated-label behavior, structured-output reliability, latency, token consumption, and estimated inference cost. The results show agents produce valid structured outputs at modest measured cost but still miss most rare severe-stress and sustained-depeg events, and the benchmark dataset and source code are released for auditing and replication.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Stablecoins are cryptocurrencies designed to maintain a fixed value, usually one US dollar, typically backed by reserves of assets; when that value deviates, it is called a depeg, which undermines their core function. Agentic AI systems are LLM-driven agents that can plan, use tools, and interact with environments over multiple steps, and evaluating them requires more than accuracy — cost, latency, and reliability matter for real deployment. Leakage-safe historical replay means testing models only on data available at each point in time, preventing future information from contaminating forecasts.

## [Dual-Stage Deep Learning Detects Dental Caries in Panoramic X-Rays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18952) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv study presents a dual-stage deep learning framework that combines Faster R-CNN for tooth localization with U-Net for pixel-wise caries segmentation in panoramic radiographs. Trained on 3,000 panoramic images using a pipeline that converts polygon annotations into binary segmentation masks, the model achieved an IoU of 0.9013, Dice coefficient of 0.9482, Recall of 0.9433, and Precision of 0.9774. Automated caries detection could improve diagnostic consistency and support clinical decision-making, especially for proximal lesions in posterior teeth that are hard to spot with traditional methods. The framework's ability to leverage algorithmically processed labels also addresses the data annotation bottleneck that limits scalability of dental AI applications. The study's systematic transformation pipeline converts large-scale polygon-annotated datasets into high-resolution binary segmentation masks, enabling pixel-wise supervised learning from both expert-verified and algorithmically processed labels. The reported metrics demonstrate superior accuracy and significantly reduced false-positive rates compared to existing methods, though the work is an incremental rather than groundbreaking contribution.

**Background**: Panoramic radiographs are two-dimensional dental X-rays that capture the entire mouth, including teeth, jaws, and surrounding structures, in a single image. Faster R-CNN is an object detection model that uses a Region Proposal Network to efficiently localize objects, while U-Net is an encoder-decoder architecture designed for pixel-level image segmentation in medical imaging. Dental caries, or tooth decay, is typically diagnosed visually or with bitewing X-rays, and early proximal lesions are often missed.

## [BadQubits: LLM Framework Detects Harmful Quantum Circuits Pre-Execution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.18965) ⭐️ 6.0/10

BadQubits is an LLM-based framework that statically detects structurally harmful OpenQASM 2.0 circuits before execution, using a dataset of 1,500 circuits (1,000 benign from MQTBench and 500 synthetic attacks). A fine-tuned Qwen Coder 2.5 7B model achieves 92.67% classification accuracy and 96.1% harmful-circuit recall, while two of four evaluated base models fail to generalize under constrained LoRA fine-tuning. This work applies LLMs to quantum circuit security, a niche but growing intersection, showing that static pre-execution screening can address physical-layer threats that dynamic inspection cannot easily catch due to measurement irreversibility and the exponential cost of classical quantum-state simulation. It also suggests that architecture-aware model selection is essential when fine-tuning LLMs for security tasks. The detector is compared against a bag-of-gates CNN under progressive confound removal and adversarial syntactic perturbation: the CNN's harmful-circuit recall drops from 100% to 17%, while the fine-tuned LLM only decreases from 96.1% to 91.2%. Correlation analysis shows model decisions track threat-defining features such as SWAP density and measurement timing rather than generator-specific artifacts like register naming.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: OpenQASM is an imperative programming language for describing quantum circuits, with version 2.0 being a widely used specification for quantum assembly. MQTBench is a quantum circuit benchmark suite providing around 70,000 benchmark circuits across different abstraction levels, used here as the source of benign programs. LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that adapts pre-trained models with far fewer trainable parameters than full fine-tuning.

## [TalkMatrix: Consistent and Diverse Character Dialogue via Joint Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19022) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2609.19022) introduces TalkMatrix, a method that formulates character dialogue generation as structured multi-prompt, multi-completion selection, where one completion is chosen per character-situation pair to jointly optimize a collection-level objective. It generates multiple candidates for every pair and selects a complete matrix using four embedding-based consistency and diversity objectives, maximizing the worst-performing objective via a two-level minimax formulation optimized with multi-start coordinate ascent. This work addresses a gap in candidate-based decoding, which typically selects completions independently per prompt and cannot satisfy global, non-decomposable requirements like cross-character consistency. It could benefit dialogue systems and creative AI applications such as role-playing and interactive storytelling, though as a niche arXiv preprint its immediate industry impact remains limited. The method is evaluated on 50 synthetic role-playing scenarios and 25 curated board game scenarios with multiple interacting characters, using an LLM-as-a-judge that rates matrix-level selection higher than random and independent cell-level selection baselines. The authors note that empirical validation remains specific to role-playing scenarios, and the discrete objective is only approximately optimized.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Candidate-based decoding is a common approach in natural language generation where a model generates several candidate completions and a selection mechanism picks one, but standard methods treat each prompt independently. Many applications, such as generating dialogue for multiple characters across situations, require a collection of outputs that jointly satisfies global constraints like consistency and diversity. Embedding-based objectives use vector representations of text to measure similarity, allowing consistency (similar lines for the same character) and diversity (distinguishable characters and situations) to be quantified.

## [AI Assistance Can Reduce Exploratory Responsiveness, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.10086) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2606.10086v2) develops a dynamical theory showing that under convergent predictive regimes, AI systems substitute for exploratory engagement, reducing a system's adaptive responsiveness and producing metastable trapping, hysteresis, premature convergence, and exploration-collapse dynamics. The paper introduces adaptive responsiveness as a central state variable and identifies contrasting exploration-enhancing regimes where AI amplifies exploratory search and adaptive mobility. The framework suggests that the long-run adaptive effects of AI depend not only on AI capability itself but also on institutional structure, developmental context, and the architecture of human-machine interaction, meaning systems with weak exploratory routines are more vulnerable to exploratory substitution. This has implications for how organizations, researchers, and policymakers design AI-assisted workflows to avoid local efficiency at the cost of global rigidity. The model formalizes adaptation over rugged epistemic landscapes with multiple locally reinforced configurations, and the effective substitution parameter is responsiveness-dependent: systems with high adaptive responsiveness may use AI to expand exploratory mobility, while those with weak routines risk exploration collapse. The paper is a theoretical preprint with no empirical validation or engagement metrics reported.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Epistemic landscapes are models that represent possible research approaches and their epistemic significance, often used to study how cognitive labor is divided in science. The exploration-exploitation tradeoff is a fundamental decision-making dilemma between choosing known best options and gathering new information, central to reinforcement learning and bandit problems. Hysteresis refers to systems whose state depends on their history, often associated with irreversible changes and path dependence in complex systems.

## [Predictive AI May Compress Exploratory Cognition, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.10094) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2606.10094v2) develops a geometric dynamical framework modeling how predictive AI systems stabilize problem-solving trajectories before exploratory search occurs, a process the author calls 'exogenous exploratory compression.' The framework yields three results: sustained predictive stabilization reduces exploratory responsiveness, curvature accumulates asymmetrically producing hysteresis and delayed recovery after assistance withdrawal, and early stabilization narrows future exploratory traversal. This work suggests that predictive AI assistance may not merely help users solve problems faster but could reshape the geometry of exploratory cognition itself, potentially reducing cognitive flexibility. It has implications for how AI tools are designed and deployed in education, decision support, and any domain where independent exploration and representational diversification matter. The framework models attention evolving over a landscape of strategies shaped by stabilizing drift, endogenous exploratory perturbation, and responsiveness-gated learning. It generates empirically testable predictions concerning exploratory entropy, premature convergence, and delayed recovery following predictive stabilization, though the abstract is truncated and no empirical validation is presented.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Classical theories of cognition describe problem solving as exploratory search through structured problem spaces, where repeated interaction gradually compresses search into efficient representational structures. This paper uses concepts from dynamical systems and geometry to analyze how such external stabilization affects later exploratory capacity.

## [HyQuant: Hybrid-Precision Quantization for LLM Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.27875) ⭐️ 6.0/10

HyQuant introduces a hybrid-precision quantization framework for LLM attention that keeps accuracy-critical vertical-line tokens and local sliding-window states in high precision while quantizing most attention states to low bits. It applies this scheme to both the Prefill stage (hybrid-precision attention operator) and the Decode stage (KV-cache compression with fused dequantization), claiming nearly lossless accuracy across diverse tasks, models, and datasets. Low-bit quantization of attention is a known bottleneck for efficient LLM inference, and HyQuant's selective high-precision retention offers a practical way to push bit-widths lower without the accuracy collapse that plagues naive quantization. If validated, this could reduce memory and compute costs for long-context serving, benefiting deployment of large models on resource-constrained hardware. The accuracy-critical regions are selected using lightweight vertical-line-aware attention-pattern signals, keeping overhead limited; in the Decode stage, KV dequantization is fused with attention computation to improve memory and hardware efficiency. The design is described as extremely simple, and code is available at the project's GitHub repository.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Quantization reduces the number of bits used to represent model weights or activations, cutting memory and compute costs for LLM training and inference. However, at very low bit-widths, activation outliers — values with unusually large magnitudes — cause large quantization errors, especially in the attention module, which is central to how Transformer models weigh different parts of the input. Existing approaches often rely on smoothing techniques to handle these outliers; HyQuant instead mixes precision levels within attention itself.

## [ED²: Dual Dynamic Index Mechanism Unifies LLM-Based Sequential Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.09253) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces ED² (End-to-End Dual Dynamic), the first LLM-based sequential recommender that adopts a dual dynamic index mechanism to unify index generation and sequential recommendation into a single LLM-backbone pipeline. It also proposes a multigrained token regulator and novel instruction tuning tasks to incorporate user-related information, achieving average improvements of 19.62% in Hit-Rate and 21.11% in NDCG on three public datasets. This work addresses a key limitation in existing LLM-based sequential recommenders, which often separate index generation from recommendation and ignore user-related information, leading to poor integration of semantic and collaborative signals. By unifying these processes, ED² could enable more effective and personalized recommendations, impacting both academic research and industrial recommender systems. The dual dynamic index mechanism allows the LLM to simultaneously generate and use dynamic indices, while the multigrained token regulator constructs alignment supervision across multiple representation granularities to enhance the LLM's comprehension. The approach is evaluated on three public datasets, showing significant improvements, but may require substantial computational resources due to the LLM backbone.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Sequential recommendation (SR) is a subfield of recommender systems that models ordered sequences of user behaviors to predict future preferences. Large language models (LLMs) have recently been applied to SR due to their semantic understanding and reasoning capabilities, but existing LLM-based SRs often treat index generation and recommendation as separate steps and neglect user-related information. The dual dynamic index mechanism aims to integrate these aspects into a unified pipeline.

A new arXiv paper (2508.06577v4) designs and tests privacy-preserving methods, including LLM-based approaches, to algorithmically shortlist projects in participatory budgeting. It shows that a naive LLM ranking based on past success has limitations, but a vote-based pipeline lets state-of-the-art LLMs perform on par with classical machine learning. The algorithms rely only on project features and anonymous historical voting data, avoiding the use of private individual data. The paper compares a naive LLM ranking approach with a vote-based pipeline, finding that user preferences in participatory budgeting are stable enough for algorithmic shortlisting to effectively approximate an initial selection of projects.

## [Constrained PSLQ Search Finds Record-Low Lehmer Measure Machin-like Identities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08307) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2508.08307v2) presents a constrained PSLQ search framework that couples the PSLQ integer-relation algorithm with number-theoretic filters based on Gaussian integers, yielding new 5- and 6-term Machin-like arctangent relations with record-low Lehmer measures of λ=1.4572 and λ=1.3291. The authors also show how these discovered relations can be algorithmically extended to generate new, longer formulas. Lower Lehmer measures directly translate to more efficient computation of π, so these new identities could improve high-precision π calculations and related numerical algorithms. The constrained PSLQ approach also offers a tractable method for future searches of low-measure relations, which is valuable to specialists in computational number theory. The search becomes tractable at large scale by using filters derived from the algebraic structure of Gaussian integers, and the resulting relations have 5 and 6 terms with Lehmer measures of 1.4572 and 1.3291 respectively. The paper also demonstrates that discovered relations can serve as a basis for generating longer formulas through algorithmic extensions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Machin-like formulas express π as a weighted sum of arctangent terms and are classical tools for computing π to high precision; their efficiency is quantified by the Lehmer measure, where a smaller value means faster convergence. PSLQ is a widely used integer-relation algorithm that finds integer coefficients satisfying a linear relation among given real numbers, and it is often applied to discover such formulas. This work combines PSLQ with number-theoretic constraints to make the search for low-measure relations more tractable.

## [NeuroSketch Offers a Practical Recipe for Neural Decoding Architectures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.09524) ⭐️ 6.0/10

NeuroSketch presents a systematic design recipe for neural decoding by comparing nine basic architectures and optimizing a CNN-2D backbone with macro- and micro-level techniques. The recipe yields two efficient models, NeuroSketch-Base (1.4M parameters) and NeuroSketch-Large (4.2M parameters), which achieve the best accuracy on each of eight tasks across visual, auditory, and speech modalities and EEG, SEEG, and ECoG signals. This work provides much-needed systematic guidance for architecture design in neural decoding, which is fundamental to brain-computer interfaces and has growing healthcare applications. By distilling design choices into a reproducible recipe, it can help researchers and practitioners build more efficient and accurate decoding models without extensive trial-and-error. The recipe combines gradual feature-map expansion and early downsampling at the macro level with grouped convolutions at the micro level, and was developed and evaluated through nearly 5,000 experiments. The two variants collectively outperform ten baselines on every task, and the code is available at https://github.com/Galaxy-Dawn/NeuroSketch.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Neural decoding is the process of translating brain signals, such as EEG, SEEG, or ECoG recordings, into meaningful outputs like movement commands or speech, and it is a core component of brain-computer interfaces (BCIs). While deep learning has improved decoding performance, there has been little systematic guidance on which neural network architectures work best for these tasks. NeuroSketch addresses this gap by empirically comparing architectures and distilling a practical design recipe.

A new arXiv paper (2601.13704v4) proposes a reparameterization technique based on feature noise injection that makes non-differentiable complexity factors—such as layer sizes and FLOP/s—differentiable, enabling joint optimization of performance and computational complexity during training with SGD. The method is validated through three case studies: a synthetic example, voice activity detection, and audio anti-spoofing, with code publicly released. This work could reduce reliance on post hoc pruning and quantization by letting models dynamically find their own size for a target performance-complexity trade-off, potentially simplifying deployment of speech models on resource-constrained devices. It also connects speech model optimization to the broader trend of differentiable neural architecture search. Unlike traditional pruning, the approach does not rely on heuristic criteria to select which weights or structures to remove; instead, model size is dynamically optimized during training. The method targets a specified performance-complexity trade-off, though the abstract does not detail the exact noise injection mechanism or its computational overhead.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Speech machine learning models are typically built with fixed layer sizes chosen heuristically, then trained to maximize task performance. Because stochastic gradient descent can only optimize differentiable functions, complexity factors like layer sizes and FLOP/s cannot be directly optimized during training, so post hoc methods such as weight quantization or model pruning are used to cut computational cost. Differentiable neural architecture search similarly transforms discrete architecture choices into continuous, differentiable optimization problems.

The friendly chatbot significantly increased communication satisfaction and was associated with higher task success than the direct chatbot, but the no-chatbot control group achieved the highest task success overall. The findings give conversational-agent designers concrete evidence that communication style affects both user satisfaction and task performance, while also warning that adding a chatbot can introduce overhead in tasks that simple instructions already handle well. This supports a task-sensitive approach to chatbot design rather than assuming chatbots always improve outcomes. The study found no significant evidence that gender moderated the effects of communication style, though exploratory gender-stratified analyses suggested patterns warranting further investigation. It also found only limited evidence of global linguistic accommodation, with selective feature-level alignment, and the between-subject design required separate participant groups per condition.

## [CzechTopic Benchmark Tests Zero-Shot Topic Localization in Historical Czech Texts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.03884) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces CzechTopic, a human-annotated benchmark for zero-shot topic localization in historical Czech documents, where models must identify text spans matching a topic defined by a name and description. The benchmark supports evaluation at both document and word levels, and the authors evaluate a range of large language models alongside BERT-based models fine-tuned on a distilled development dataset. Topic localization is a relatively underexplored task, and this benchmark extends it to a low-resource historical language, providing a public dataset and evaluation framework that could spur further research on historical document analysis and zero-shot NLP. The finding that smaller distilled models remain competitive with LLMs is relevant for practitioners working under compute constraints. Evaluation is performed relative to human agreement rather than a single reference annotation, acknowledging annotator variability. Results show substantial variability among LLMs, ranging from near-human topic detection to pronounced failures in span localization, while distilled token embedding models remain competitive despite their smaller scale; the dataset and framework are available at https://github.com/dcgm/czechtopic.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Topic localization is the task of finding the exact spans of text that express a given topic, which is defined by a name and a description. Zero-shot means the model must handle topics it was not explicitly trained on, typically by comparing the topic description with the input text in natural language. Historical Czech documents pose additional challenges due to archaic spelling, vocabulary, and formatting, making them a useful stress test for modern NLP models. The benchmark's use of human agreement reflects standard practice in NLP annotation, where inter-annotator agreement is used to assess data reliability.

## [EfficientTDMPC Boosts Sample Efficiency in Continuous Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.16692) ⭐️ 6.0/10

EfficientTDMPC introduces an ensemble of dynamics models that averages return estimates across models and rollout depths, plus an optional uncertainty penalty in the planner objective, along with practical improvements to buffer data freshness and reduced compute. The authors report state-of-the-art sample efficiency on HumanoidBench-Hard and DMC hard in the low-data regime, while matching SOTA on DMC easy. Sample efficiency is a central bottleneck for applying reinforcement learning to real-world continuous control, where each environment interaction is costly. By improving the TD-MPC family's planner objective and enabling better use of higher update-to-data (UTD) ratios, this work could make model-based RL more practical for robotics and other domains with limited data. The method reduces return estimation error in two ways: averaging across an ensemble of dynamics models and across different rollout depths, and optionally penalizing uncertain return estimates so the planner avoids risky actions. These contributions let EfficientTDMPC benefit more from a higher UTD ratio, though the gains are demonstrated primarily in low-data regimes of specific benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: TD-MPC is a model-based reinforcement learning algorithm that performs local trajectory optimization in the latent space of a learned, decoder-free world model, using model predictive control (MPC) to plan actions. Model-based RL can be far more sample-efficient than model-free methods but often suffers from compounding errors in the learned model and value estimates. Ensemble dynamics models are a common technique to quantify epistemic uncertainty, where disagreement among models guides exploration or risk-averse planning.

## [TDPM: Time-Aware Diffusion with Preference Disentanglement for Generative Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.01670) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces TDPM, a generative recommendation framework that applies time-aware diffusion on semantic ID (SID) tokens, explicitly disentangling user preference into a long-term period preference and a short-term point preference triggered by recent focal events. Experiments on three public real-world datasets show average improvements of up to 29.21% in HR@20 and 25.45% in NDCG@20 over state-of-the-art baselines. Existing diffusion-based generative recommenders apply a uniform diffusion process to all historical interactions, ignoring that user preferences evolve non-stationarily over time; TDPM addresses this gap and could improve recommendation accuracy in real-world systems where short-term intent and long-term taste must both be captured. TDPM operates on SID tokens rather than raw item IDs, and an ablation study confirms that time-aware token diffusion is necessary for the performance gains; however, the evaluation is limited to three public datasets and the paper reports no community discussion or deployment results.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Generative Recommenders (GRs) replace traditional item IDs with semantic indices (SIDs), typically produced by tokenizers such as RQ-VAE, and then train sequence-to-sequence models to generate the next item. Diffusion models, known for strong generative capability, have recently been adopted as backbones for GRs, but they treat all historical interactions uniformly. User preference is shaped by multifaceted time-evolving factors and thus exhibits a non-stationary distribution over time, which motivates disentangling it into stable and transient components.

## [LargeMonitor Uses Pretrained Models for Task-Free Continual Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.09430) ⭐️ 6.0/10

LargeMonitor is a new framework that decouples drift detection from training dynamics in online task-free continual learning (TFCL) by using frozen large vision models (LVMs) for zero-shot drift detection and large multimodal models (LMMs) for diagnosing the semantic cause of drift. It then dynamically selects shift-specific optimization strategies, and experiments across multiple TFCL benchmarks show improved performance for existing online TFCL algorithms. Online TFCL is critical for agents that must learn from non-stationary data streams without task boundaries, and LargeMonitor's decoupled detection could make such systems more robust and adaptive. By improving existing algorithms without replacing them, it offers a practical enhancement for real-world continual learning deployments. The framework uses a frozen, stable representation space of LVMs to avoid brittle threshold tuning and training-dependent interference, and it distinguishes between novel class emergence and environmental domain shift via LMMs. However, as an arXiv preprint, it lacks peer review and community engagement metrics, so its broader impact remains unclear.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Online task-free continual learning (TFCL) requires a model to learn from a single pass over a non-stationary data stream without explicit task identifiers. Existing methods often rely on parameter-efficient prompt tuning or dynamic structure expansion driven by training-coupled signals like loss fluctuations, which can be brittle and agnostic to the true cause of distribution drift. Large pretrained foundation models, such as large vision models (LVMs) and large multimodal models (LMMs), provide stable representations and semantic understanding that can be leveraged for more robust drift detection and diagnosis.

## [New Reward Model Debias Text-to-Image Evaluation via Cultural Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15740) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new paper introduces an Implicit Cultural Alignment Reward Model built on a 4.2-billion-parameter Multimodal Large Language Model (MLLM) that integrates an Implicit Cultural Probe with a Skip-connection Cross-Attention (SkipCA) mechanism. Evaluated on 3,323 image pairs from the CulturalFrames benchmark, it achieves 83.49% pairwise accuracy and processes each evaluation in 0.21 seconds, a 10x speedup over standard VQA-based evaluators. This work addresses a critical gap in text-to-image evaluation: existing metrics often miss implicit cultural norms, leading to biased judgments that can propagate through preference optimization pipelines like RLHF and DPO. By providing a fast, culturally aware scalar reward, it could help make generative AI fairer and more trustworthy across diverse cultural contexts. The SkipCA mechanism allows late-stage semantic features to directly attend to early-stage visual representations, preserving culturally salient details that standard models might overlook. The model bypasses autoregressive text generation, enabling real-time reward modeling, and outperforms representative vision-language metrics with Pearson and Kendall correlations of 0.5268 and 0.3749, respectively.

**Background**: Text-to-Image (T2I) systems generate images from text prompts, and evaluating their cultural authenticity is increasingly important for fair generative AI. Traditional evaluation metrics and multimodal judges rely on visual-semantic representations that underrepresent implicit cultural norms, while VQA-based evaluators depend on slow autoregressive text generation. Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) integrate language reasoning with vision encoders, and Skip-connection Cross-Attention (SkipCA) is a family of attention-guided skip fusion mechanisms that actively integrate encoder features into the decoder.

## [LPIFM: A Learned Human-Preference Metric for Infrared-Visible Image Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01301) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced LPIFM (Learned Perceptual Image Fusion Measure), among the earliest learned fusion assessors trained directly on dense human A/B/Tie comparisons, and publicly released a dataset of 6,300 unordered comparisons across 25 methods on 21 VIFB scenes. LPIFM achieves 79.2-84.0% agreement with human pairwise judgments and Spearman correlations of 0.941-0.977 with human-derived method rankings, exceeding the strongest of 19 conventional metrics by 16.3-21.1 percentage points on full method pools. Infrared-visible image fusion is widely used in surveillance, remote sensing, and autonomous perception, yet evaluating fusion quality has long relied on conventional metrics that correlate poorly with human perception. A scalable, human-aligned learned measure could standardize benchmarking and guide the development of fusion algorithms toward outputs that people actually judge as better. LPIFM jointly examines both source images and both fused candidates using a shared hierarchical encoder, triadic interaction, and a tie-aware objective that predicts both preference and perceptual indifference. Consistency diagnostics show 99.98-100% candidate-swap agreement and no observed decisive preference cycles, and on the external EVAFusion protocol it surpasses all 19 conventional metrics after only three epochs of fine-tuning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Sep 17, 04:00

**Background**: Infrared-visible image fusion (IVIF) combines thermal infrared and visible-light images into a single image that preserves complementary information, such as heat signatures and texture detail. VIFB is a widely used benchmark providing 21 registered visible-infrared image pairs covering indoor, outdoor, low-illumination, and over-exposure conditions. Conventional fusion metrics are hand-designed and often disagree with how humans perceive fusion quality, which motivates learning assessment directly from human pairwise comparisons.
